- Legend says a knight built it to atone for killing his wife ('mal muerta', wrongly killed). The real 1448 crime happened after the tower was already standing. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torre_de_la_Malmuerta_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Anecdote: another legend says that a rider who could read the whole inscription while galloping under the arch would bring the tower down and win a hidden treasure. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torre_de_la_Malmuerta_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- It later served as a prison for nobles, an astronomer's observatory (Gonzalo Antonio Serrano) and the seat of the chess federation. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torre_de_la_Malmuerta_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* Traffic junction, so cross with care. A good riddle hook: the 'badly killed' legend.

**2. Santa Marina church and the Manolete monument** · Plaza del Conde de Priego / Plaza de Santa Marina, Córdoba · 37.88994032, -4.77467388

- Santa Marina is one of the 'Fernandine' churches built after Fernando III conquered Córdoba; it is mentioned by 1256 and is among the oldest of them. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Marina_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- It gives its name to one of the city's most traditional quarters, nicknamed the 'barrio de los toreros' (bullfighters' quarter). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Marina_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The Manolete monument by sculptor Manuel Álvarez Laviada and architect Luis Moya was unveiled on 8 May 1956. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monumento_a_Manolete_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Anecdote: when public donations fell short, a charity bullfight on 21 October 1951 raised 800,000 pesetas for the monument. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monumento_a_Manolete_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- A fire on 23 June 1880 badly damaged the church; worship resumed on 17 July 1882. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Marina_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* Coordinates are the church; the Manolete monument stands in the square in front (Wikipedia: 37.88968611, -4.77563611).

**3. Cristo de los Faroles, Plaza de Capuchinos** · Plaza de Capuchinos, Córdoba (top of the Cuesta del Bailío) · 37.88817, -4.77761

- The monument was made in 1794 by Córdoba sculptor Juan Navarro León, promoted by the Capuchin friar Diego José de Cádiz. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Faroles_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The Christ is white marble and the cross is black; its popular name comes from the eight lanterns around it. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Faroles_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The pedestal is engraved with the Miserere psalm; the cross bears a Latin verse meaning 'And the rock was Christ'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Faroles_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Anecdote: the square has been a film set many times, including Franco Zeffirelli's 'Callas Forever' (2002). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Faroles_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* Very quiet and atmospheric at dusk. Leave via the Cuesta del Bailío stairs: 32 steps, nine wooden crosses on the wall, and a 1924 tile of the Virgen de los Dolores where Manolete is said to have prayed before fights. These are good extra lesson facts, but the stairs are only ~80 m from this stop, too close for a separate stop. Steps make this leg not step-free. Source: https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuesta_del_Bail%C3%ADo_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29

**4. Iglesia de San Agustín** · Plaza de San Agustín, Córdoba · 37.8892883, -4.77171994

- Building began in 1328; the main chapel was founded in 1335. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Agust%C3%ADn_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The Augustinian monks moved here when King Alfonso XI began building the Alcázar de los Reyes Cristianos on their earlier site. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Agust%C3%ADn_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- During the French invasion the convent became a barracks and the church a granary and stable. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Agust%C3%ADn_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Anecdote: after the Civil War damage, an angel falling from the vaults forced the church to close again. A €3.6 million restoration reopened it on 9 October 2009. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Agust%C3%ADn_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* Wikipedia says the tower has 'two bodies of bells', which supports the answer, but I have not confirmed how it reads from the plaza, so it is 'likely'. The square has bar terraces. The leg from stop 3 goes down the Bailío steps and along Calle Alfaros / Calle Morería area; check.

**5. Iglesia de San Lorenzo** · Plaza de San Lorenzo, Córdoba · 37.88855855, -4.76889366

- A Fernandine church, probably built between 1244 and 1300 on the site of the mosque of al-Mugira; remains of its minaret survive inside. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Lorenzo_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Its large Gothic-Mudéjar rose window is one of the finest of the period. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Lorenzo_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The bell tower was built in 1555 by Hernán Ruiz II over the old minaret. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Lorenzo_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- After a fire in 1687 the wall paintings were covered; a 1963 restoration brought back the 14th-century paintings and the rose window. The three porch arches had been reopened in 1929. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Lorenzo_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* A three-arched porch is unusual in Andalusia, so it makes a good look-up question. Bus stops on nearby Calle María Auxiliadora (not verified).

**6. Iglesia de San Andrés** · Calle del Realejo / Plaza de San Andrés, Córdoba · 37.88593418, -4.7730268

- The church is first mentioned in 1246, ten years after the Christian conquest, and was built over the remains of the basilica of San Zoilo, beside the Roman Via Augusta. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- In the early 18th century the old church became the transept of a new one, turning its orientation from east-west to north-south (finished 1755). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The Baroque main door on Calle del Realejo shows the arms of Bishop Marcelino Siuri, and above it a niche with Saint Andrew holding a book and his X-shaped cross. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- The old Renaissance doorway was bricked up until 1928; today it can be seen again on Calle Fernán Pérez de Oliva. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Andr%C3%A9s_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* Two façades: make sure players are at the Baroque one on Calle del Realejo.

**7. Basílica de San Pedro** · Plaza de San Pedro, Córdoba · 37.8825997, -4.77183253

- A paleochristian church holding the relics of three Córdoba martyrs is thought to have stood here. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_San_Pedro_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- In 1575 the remains of the Martyrs of Córdoba were found beneath the church. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_San_Pedro_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Hernán Ruiz II built a new portal in 1542; the remodelled main façade kept the original rose window. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_San_Pedro_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Pope Benedict XVI made it a minor basilica in 2006. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_San_Pedro_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- Anecdote: the famous image-maker Juan de Mesa was baptised here in 1583. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_San_Pedro_%28C%C3%B3rdoba%29))
- *Note:* The rose window is sourced; marked 'likely' because I have not confirmed it is clearly visible from the plaza. The Plaza de la Corredera is about 250 m away, a good place to finish for a drink.

</details>


---

## Granada

**Visitors:** The Alhambra received 2,615,188 visitors in 2016 (second most-visited site in Spain after the Sagrada Familia). No city-wide figure was found in the sources I could reach. (2016) ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alhambra)) *From Spanish Wikipedia; this is an old figure. Update from the Patronato de la Alhambra or INE.*

**Official walking routes:** Not found: the web-search budget ran out before I could check the city tourism site (Granada Turismo) for published walking routes

**Notes:** Granada sits at 680 m, with cold winters and hot summers (transitional Mediterranean / cold semi-arid climate). The Albaicín is steep and cobbled, so the classic route has a real climb. Spring festivities: Semana Santa and Corpus Christi. Sierra Nevada skiing brings winter visitors. Alhambra tickets sell out, so these routes avoid needing one. Source: https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granada. Research note: the web-search budget ran out, so official routes and competition are unverified and most facts come from Spanish Wikipedia.

### The Darro Route · classic

*From the market square up the river to the best Alhambra view in town.*

Plaza de Bib-Rambla → Puerta de Elvira · 7 stops · ≈ 2.5 km · 2.5 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** A one-way line through Nasrid, royal and Albaicín Granada that ends at the San Nicolás view of the Alhambra, with no Alhambra ticket needed.

**Already offered:** Not verified (the web-search budget ran out). Albaicín and San Nicolás walks are offered by most free-tour companies; check granadatur.com and the free-tour sites.

**To fix:** a 111 m leg (geofences may overlap); 3 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Plaza de Bib-Rambla | The central fountain is also named after the sea god standing on top. Who is he? | Neptune | likely |
| 2 | Capilla Real, Calle Oficios | Find the emblems of the Catholic Monarchs carved on the chapel: a yoke and a bundle of what? | Arrows | check |
| 3 | Corral del Carbón | In the middle of the courtyard, how many spouts does the stone basin have? | 2 | sourced |
| 4 | Plaza Nueva and the church of Santa Ana | Above the three statue niches on Santa Ana's doorway, a round medallion shows whom? | The Virgin and Child | sourced |
| 5 | San Pedro y San Pablo, Carrera del Darro | How many saints are carved in the niche above the main door? | 2 | sourced |
| 6 | Mirador de San Nicolás | Look past the Alhambra. Which mountain range rises behind it? | Sierra Nevada | likely |
| 7 | Puerta de Elvira | What shape is the gate's great arch? | Horseshoe | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Plaza de Bib-Rambla** · Plaza de Bib-Rambla, Granada · 37.17518539, -3.59979232

- The square dates back to Nasrid times, next to the Alcaicería and the Zacatín, the city's old trading heart; later it hosted jousts and bullfights. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Bib-Rambla))
- The Fuente de los Gigantones, or Neptune fountain, was carved for the convent of the Agustinos Calzados. After the convent was demolished it moved to the Paseo del Salón, then the Paseo de la Bomba (1892), and here in 1940. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuente_de_los_Gigantones))
- Anecdote: around 1499-1500 Cardinal Cisneros had the library of the Madraza carried to this square and burned in a public bonfire. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madraza_de_Granada))
- The flower kiosks around the fountain change through the year: firs and poinsettias at Christmas, bouquets for Valentine's and Mother's Day. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Bib-Rambla))
- *Note:* The name 'de Neptuno' is sourced; marked 'likely' because I have not confirmed that the top figure is what gives the name. Flower stalls are not stable, so don't use them for questions. Many café terraces.

**2. Capilla Real, Calle Oficios** · Calle Oficios, Granada (between the Capilla Real and the Madraza) · 37.17598611, -3.59903611

- Isabel and Fernando chose Granada for their tomb; a royal decree of 13 September 1504 created the Royal Chapel, built 1505-1517 by Enrique Egas in late Gothic style. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capilla_Real_de_Granada))
- Buried here are the Catholic Monarchs, their daughter Juana I and her husband Felipe el Hermoso. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capilla_Real_de_Granada))
- Across the lane, the Madraza was the first public madrasa of al-Andalus, opened in 1349 by the Nasrid king Yusuf I; it taught law, medicine and mathematics. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madraza_de_Granada))
- The Lonja next door, built in 1518 in the corner between the chapel and the Sagrario, was used for banking and trade. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capilla_Real_de_Granada))
- *Note:* The yoke-and-arrows shield is sourced on the chapel's Gothic portal, but that portal may face the cathedral interior or the ticketed area. I could not confirm these emblems are visible from Calle Oficios, so this is 'check'. Interior ticketed. The leg from stop 1 is short (~110 m straight, ~180 m on foot via Calle Libreros/Oficios); check it meets 150 m.

**3. Corral del Carbón** · Corral del Carbón, Calle Mariana Pineda, Granada (by Calle Reyes Católicos; verify address) · 37.17492722, -3.59797634

- It is the only Nasrid alhóndiga (merchants' inn) preserved complete in the Iberian Peninsula; it was built before 1336 and called al-Funduq al-Yadida, the 'New Inn'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corral_del_Carb%C3%B3n))
- Travelling merchants slept upstairs and stored and sold goods wholesale; a little bridge over the Darro linked it to the market. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corral_del_Carb%C3%B3n))
- Anecdote: after 1531 it lodged coal-men, hence 'Corral del Carbón'; then it was a comedy theatre (until 1593) and later a tenement. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corral_del_Carb%C3%B3n))
- Threatened with demolition, it was bought by the State in 1933 and restored by Leopoldo Torres Balbás. Today it is the seat of the Granada International Festival of Music and Dance. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corral_del_Carb%C3%B3n))
- *Note:* The courtyard is usually open and free, but opening hours were not checked. Bonus look-up: the twin window above the great arch and the Kufic inscription band. The Plaza de Isabel la Católica is ~100 m away; it is used as the start of the local route.

**4. Plaza Nueva and the church of Santa Ana** · Plaza de Santa Ana / east end of Plaza Nueva, Granada · 37.17722222, -3.59472222

- Santa Ana was begun in 1537 to a design by Diego de Siloé, on the site of the Almanzora mosque; its slim brick tower dates from 1561-1563. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Ana_%28Granada%29))
- The Renaissance doorway (1542-1547) by Sebastián and Juan de Alcántara has Corinthian columns, three niches with statues and a medallion of the Virgin and Child. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Ana_%28Granada%29))
- Anecdote: the heroine Mariana Pineda, executed under Fernando VII, was married in this church. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santa_Ana_%28Granada%29))
- Plaza Nueva is built largely over the vaulted-over river Darro. On its east side stands the Real Chancillería palace (1531-1587), the first building in Spain built to house a court of justice. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_Nueva_%28Granada%29))
- The Chancillería court, moved here in 1505, originally covered the territory south of the river Tajo; it was abolished in 1834. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Chanciller%C3%ADa_de_Granada))
- *Note:* Busy square, with Alhambra minibuses and taxis. The Carrera del Darro starts here: a narrow road with some traffic and minibuses.

**5. San Pedro y San Pablo, Carrera del Darro** · Carrera del Darro, Granada (opposite the Casa de Castril) · 37.17855, -3.59168

- The church was designed by Juan de Maeda and built 1559-1567, on the site of an earlier church which had itself replaced the Mosque of the Baths. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Pedro_y_San_Pablo_%28Granada%29))
- The Renaissance doorway (Pedro de Orea, 1589) has a niche with stone statues of Saints Peter and Paul between pairs of Corinthian columns. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Pedro_y_San_Pablo_%28Granada%29))
- From the entrance you look straight up at the Tajo de la Alhambra and the towers of the Alcazaba. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Pedro_y_San_Pablo_%28Granada%29))
- On the way you pass El Bañuelo (Carrera del Darro 31), an 11th-century Zirid bathhouse, one of the best-preserved Arab public baths in Spain. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Ba%C3%B1uelo))
- *Note:* El Bañuelo is a ticketed monument, so it is mentioned rather than used for a question. The Paseo de los Tristes is just beyond. The next leg climbs steeply (about 0.6 km on foot), via the Cuesta del Chapiz or the lanes behind; plan it so families can manage.

**6. Mirador de San Nicolás** · Plaza Mirador de San Nicolás, Albaicín, Granada · 37.1813339, -3.59253244

- The Mudéjar church of San Nicolás was built in 1525; the viewpoint square on its south side has a famous panorama of the Alhambra. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Nicol%C3%A1s_%28Granada%29))
- In 1932 arsonists burned the church's precious Mudéjar roof; the vault collapsed three years later, and the current wooden roof dates from 1947. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Nicol%C3%A1s_%28Granada%29))
- Beside the church, in the Plaza del Cementerio de San Nicolás, is an old aljibe (water cistern). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Nicol%C3%A1s_%28Granada%29))
- The Albaicín was added to the UNESCO World Heritage listing of the Alhambra and Generalife in 1994. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albaic%C3%ADn))
- *Note:* Coordinates are those of the church (Wikipedia); the mirador is its south side. Very crowded at sunset. The 'Sierra Nevada' answer is well known but not sourced here. Albaicín minibuses stop nearby (check line).

**7. Puerta de Elvira** · Puerta de Elvira, north-west end of Calle Elvira, Granada · 37.18214277, -3.59961789

- It was the main entrance to Granada under Islamic rule, on the road to Madinat Ilbira (Medina Elvira), which gave it its name. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_Elvira))
- Built by the Zirid sultans and remodelled under the Nasrid Yusuf I, it became a fortress with four towers, three barbicans and two inner gates. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_Elvira))
- Anecdote: in 1612 the barbicans were knocked down and twelve houses were built against the wall; they survive almost unchanged. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_Elvira))
- It has been a National Historic-Artistic Monument since 1896; Lorca mentions the Arco de Elvira in his 'Gacela del mercado matutino'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_Elvira))
- *Note:* Downhill finish from the Albaicín via the Cuesta de la Alhacaba (steep cobbles; ~0.8 km on foot, check). Buses on the Gran Vía / Avenida de la Constitución nearby.

</details>

### The Realejo Route · local

*Heroes, potters and pomegranates in Granada's old Jewish quarter.*

Plaza de Isabel la Católica → Puerta de las Granadas (Cuesta de Gomérez) · 7 stops · ≈ 2.9 km · 2.5 h · **ready to write**

**Why:** The Realejo (old potters' and Jewish quarter) is a lived-in barrio of squares, palaces and gardens that most Alhambra visitors skip.

**Already offered:** Not verified (search budget ran out). Realejo street-art and Jewish-quarter tours exist in Granada; check before launch.

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Plaza de Isabel la Católica | In the bronze group, who is presenting his plans to the queen? | Christopher Columbus | sourced |
| 2 | Casa de los Tiros | How many life-size hero statues stand on the tower's stone façade? | 5 | sourced |
| 3 | Iglesia de Santo Domingo | Which two letters are carved between the arches of the stone porch? | F and Y | sourced |
| 4 | Cuarto Real de Santo Domingo | Inside the tower hall, what colours are the glazed tiles of the wall skirting? | Green and blue | sourced |
| 5 | Campo del Príncipe: Cristo de los Favores | Read the inscription at the foot of the stone cross. What year does it give? | 1682 | sourced |
| 6 | Carmen de los Mártires | What lies in the middle of the romantic garden's lake? | An island | sourced |
| 7 | Puerta de las Granadas | How many pomegranates crown the top of the gate? | 3 | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Plaza de Isabel la Católica** · Plaza de Isabel la Católica, Granada (Gran Vía de Colón / Calle Reyes Católicos) · 37.17564444, -3.59750556

- The bronze group by Mariano Benlliure was made in Rome in 1892. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Isabel_la_Cat%C3%B3lica_%28Granada%29))
- It shows Queen Isabel accepting Columbus' proposals at the Capitulaciones de Santa Fe, signed in 1492. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Isabel_la_Cat%C3%B3lica_%28Granada%29))
- The statues were brought here from the Paseo del Salón in the early 1960s; the square itself was laid out in 1962. Locals simply call it 'Colón'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_Isabel_la_Cat%C3%B3lica_%28Granada%29))
- *Note:* Central, flat start; many buses on the Gran Vía and Reyes Católicos.

**2. Casa de los Tiros** · Calle Pavaneras, Realejo, Granada · 37.17481956, -3.59538101

- The house is named after the artillery pieces (tiros) on its battlements; it was part of the wall of the old potters' quarter, hence its fortress look. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_los_Tiros))
- The five heroes on the façade are Hercules, Theseus, Mercury, Jason and Hector, all with feet apart as if ready for battle. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_los_Tiros))
- Above the door a sword pierces a heart, with the motto 'El (corazón) manda' ('the heart commands'). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_los_Tiros))
- Anecdote: its first owners, the Granada Venegas, descended from the Nasrid king Yusuf IV through Cidi Yahya, who converted and became Pedro de Granada. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_los_Tiros))
- *Note:* It is a museum inside (entry conditions not checked); the question is answered from the street. Narrow pavement on Calle Pavaneras.

**3. Iglesia de Santo Domingo** · Plaza de Santo Domingo, Realejo, Granada · 37.1730264, -3.5945504

- Begun in 1512 as part of the Dominican convent of Santa Cruz la Real, it started Gothic and was completed in later styles. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santo_Domingo_%28Granada%29))
- The stone porch has three round arches; between them are the initials 'F' and 'Y' of the Catholic Monarchs, Fernando and Ysabel. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santo_Domingo_%28Granada%29))
- The painted façade shows a niche with the Virgen del Rosario, co-patron of Granada, and the royal arms with the motto 'Tanto monta'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santo_Domingo_%28Granada%29))
- The square in front is presided over by a monument to the writer Fray Luis de Granada. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_Santo_Domingo_%28Granada%29))
- *Note:* Good shady square. Painted façade elements could fade; the carved initials are the stable detail.

**4. Cuarto Real de Santo Domingo** · Cuarto Real de Santo Domingo, Cuesta de Aixa area, Realejo, Granada (entrance to verify) · 37.17152333, -3.59499026

- The Nasrid emir Muhammad II (1273-1302) walled the potters' quarter; one of its towers holds a qubba, a royal reception hall. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuarto_Real_de_Santo_Domingo))
- After 1492 Sultana Aixa sold the estate to the Catholic Monarchs, who gave it to the Dominicans for their convent. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuarto_Real_de_Santo_Domingo))
- The City Council bought it in 1990; excavations found the foundations of the portico, the pool and the garden paths. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuarto_Real_de_Santo_Domingo))
- The double-height hall is compared to the Alhambra's Hall of the Ambassadors, with muqarnas, a twin window, plasterwork and a tiled dado. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuarto_Real_de_Santo_Domingo))
- *Note:* Access is not verified: opening hours and whether entry is free must be checked (the brief needs free access). If it is closed or ticketed, drop this stop; the legs on either side still work. The garden exterior may offer a substitute detail.

**5. Campo del Príncipe: Cristo de los Favores** · Campo del Príncipe, Realejo, Granada · 37.17331, -3.592111

- The square was levelled in 1497 for the wedding celebrations of Prince Juan, son of the Catholic Monarchs; it was built on an old Muslim cemetery. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realejo-San_Mat%C3%ADas))
- Realejo neighbours paid for the cross in 1640 for another square; it was moved here in 1682, as the inscription 'SE A CABO AÑO DE 1682' at its foot records. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Favores))
- Its base is an urn with reliefs of the four Evangelists. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cristo_de_los_Favores))
- Anecdote: every Good Friday at 3 pm thousands of Granadinos gather here in silence to ask the Christ for three favours. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realejo-San_Mat%C3%ADas))
- *Note:* No source coordinates. A lively local square with bars. The leg to stop 6 climbs steadily up to the Carmen de los Mártires; check the walking line (Cuesta del Caidero / Paseo de los Mártires).

**6. Carmen de los Mártires** · Paseo de los Mártires, Granada · 37.1719652, -3.58628021

- At more than seven hectares, it is the largest carmen (villa with garden) in Granada, with views of Sierra Nevada, the Vega and the city. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_de_los_M%C3%A1rtires))
- Saint John of the Cross was prior of the Carmelite convent here from 1582 to 1588 and wrote most of his works here. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_de_los_M%C3%A1rtires))
- The convent was destroyed in 1842; in 1891 the Belgian mining engineer Humbert Meersmans turned the pond into a romantic lake with an island and waterfalls. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_de_los_M%C3%A1rtires))
- Anecdote: it was given to the city in 1958; in the 1970s a hotel was planned on part of it, but public pressure stopped the works. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_de_los_M%C3%A1rtires))
- *Note:* A municipal garden; free entry and opening hours not verified (check before launch). The lake's black swans are animals, so don't use them for questions. Uphill approach.

**7. Puerta de las Granadas** · Top of the Cuesta de Gomérez, Granada (entrance to the Alhambra woods) · 37.17583333, -3.59305556

- The gate was built to mark Emperor Charles V's 1526 visit to Granada after his wedding in Seville, under Pedro Machuca, architect of the Palace of Charles V. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_las_Granadas))
- It replaced an Islamic gate, the Bib al-Buxar ('gate of good news'). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_las_Granadas))
- Three split-open pomegranates crown the pediment, above the imperial arms with figures of Peace and Abundance; you walk through one of three round arches. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_de_las_Granadas))
- Nearby on the Mauror hill are the Torres Bermejas ('vermilion towers'), named after their colour; the Jewish quarter around them, Garnata al-Yahud, may have given the whole city its name. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realejo-San_Mat%C3%ADas))
- *Note:* Free: these are the public Alhambra woods; no Alhambra ticket is needed. The walk from stop 6 goes past Torres Bermejas and the Hotel Alhambra Palace (1910). Finish downhill on the Cuesta de Gomérez to Plaza Nueva (~350 m). Source on Torres Bermejas: https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torres_Bermejas

</details>


---

## Málaga

**Visitors:** Málaga city closed 2024 with about 1.6 million hotel guests and almost 3.5 million overnight stays (INE hotel occupancy survey, reported by the city council on 12 Feb 2025). 2023: 1,517,106 hotel guests, of whom 59.7% (904,751) were international. 2025: about 1.83 million hotel guests per a later council note. Hotel guests only; total visitors (cruise, holiday flats, day-trippers) are higher. ([source](https://www.malaga.eu/visorcontenido/ANUDocumentDisplayer/174605/COMUNICADO.pdf))

**Official walking routes:** [Málaga in two days: the essentials (Visita Málaga blog)](https://visita.malaga.eu/en/what-to-see-and-do/blog/malaga-in-two-days-the-essentials-to-discover-and-fall-in-love-with-the-city-p1885); [Five walks to enjoy August sunsets in Málaga](https://visita.malaga.eu/en/what-to-see-and-do/blog/five-walks-to-enjoy-august-sunsets-in-malaga-p2590); [Nature in Málaga: routes to enjoy autumn (Gibralfaro, Guadalhorce, coastal path)](https://visita.malaga.eu/en/what-to-see-and-do/blog/nature-in-malaga-routes-to-enjoy-autumn-in-the-city-p2039); [Tourist guides directory (official)](https://visita.malaga.eu/en/plan/tourist-information/tourist-guides)

**Notes:** Hot summers (July-August): schedule walks early or late, and both routes have long sunny stretches. Semana Santa (Holy Week) fills the centre with stands and processions, and Plaza de la Constitución gets a large grandstand. The Feria de Málaga in August puts casetas in the centre. Christmas lights on Calle Larios draw very dense crowds from late November. The centre is flat; Gibralfaro hill is the only real climb, and neither route goes up it. Cruise-ship days crowd the cathedral, Calle Larios and Muelle Uno.

### The Manquita Route · classic

*From a Nasrid gate to the lighthouse with a woman's name: 2,000 years of Málaga in one stroll.*

Mercado Central de Atarazanas (Calle Atarazanas 10) → La Farola lighthouse (Paseo de la Farola) · 7 stops · ≈ 3.0 km · 2.0 h · **ready to write**

**Why:** It strings the must-sees (market, cathedral, Picasso's square, Roman theatre, port and lighthouse) on one flat line from the old town to the sea.

**Already offered:** Yes. Free walking tours cover most of the old-town part (Plaza de la Marina, Larios, cathedral, Merced, Alcazabilla/Roman theatre), e.g. GuruWalk https://www.guruwalk.com/walks/38739-free-tour-malaga-explore-the-historic-center-with-official-and-local-guides and Civitatis https://www.civitatis.com/en/malaga/free-walking-tour/ . None found as a self-guided game, and none continues to Muelle Uno and La Farola.

**To fix:** 1 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mercado Central de Atarazanas | The market's main entrance is an old Nasrid (Moorish) marble gate. What shape is its big arch? | Horseshoe arch | sourced |
| 2 | Plaza de la Constitución | Steel plates set into the paving show newspaper front pages from the year Spain's current Constitution was approved. Which year? | 1978 | sourced |
| 3 | Catedral de Málaga, 'La Manquita' | Look up at the main façade. How many of its two planned bell towers were actually finished? | One | sourced |
| 4 | Plaza de la Merced (Picasso's square) | Find the bronze Pablo Picasso. What is he doing? | Sitting on a bench | sourced |
| 5 | Teatro Romano (from Calle Alcazabilla) | The visitor centre next to the theatre is a glass-and-steel box with Roman text on its outer walls. Which Roman law is reproduced? | Lex Flavia Malacitana | sourced |
| 6 | Centre Pompidou Málaga (El Cubo) | The Pompidou's multicoloured glass skylight on the quay has a nickname that describes its shape. What is it? | The Cube | sourced |
| 7 | La Farola | La Farola is famous as one of the few lighthouses in Spain with a 'female' name. In what century was this lighthouse finished? | 19th century | check |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Mercado Central de Atarazanas** · Calle Atarazanas 10, 29005 Málaga · 36.71844043, -4.42406728

- The iron market was built from 1876 to 1879 by architect Joaquín de Rucoba, on the site of a Nasrid shipyard (atarazana), which gives it its name. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_de_Atarazanas))
- The only survivor of the shipyard is a marble gate: a pointed horseshoe arch, moved about 25 m forward from its original position. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_de_Atarazanas))
- Shields on the arch date it to the reign of Nasrid sultan Muhammad V (1354-1391). The building's façade once faced the sea. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_de_Atarazanas))
- The iron came from Seville's San Antonio foundry, which also built Seville's Triana bridge. Like other Spanish iron markets, it was inspired by Les Halles in Paris. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_de_Atarazanas))
- Inside is a 1973 stained-glass window by the Atienza brothers, with 108 panels showing city monuments. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercado_de_Atarazanas))
- *Note:* Free to enter; closed Sundays. The question is about the outside gate, so it works at any hour. Busy late morning. The Atarazanas Metro station is nearby; check it is open.

**2. Plaza de la Constitución** · Plaza de la Constitución (top of Calle Marqués de Larios) · 36.72129889, -4.4222075

- This was Málaga's main square from the 15th century: the town hall stood here until 1869, along with the jail and the court. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- It was first called Plaza de la Constitución in 1812, after 'La Pepa'. Later names were Libertad, República Federal and José Antonio Primo de Rivera, before it got its original name back. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- In 2004 stainless-steel plates went into the paving, showing front pages from after the 1978 Constitution was approved. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- The marble Fuente de Génova (Carlos V fountain) came from Genoa. It was moved to the Parque and then brought back in 2002, when Calle Larios was pedestrianised. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- Pasaje Heredia (1837), off the square, was Spain's first commercial arcade. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- *Note:* The plates can be hidden by the Semana Santa grandstand, Christmas events or Feria casetas. Check that the plates are still legible.

**3. Catedral de Málaga, 'La Manquita'** · Calle Molina Lario 9 / Plaza del Obispo · 36.720042, -4.42012

- Building began in 1528 and stopped, unfinished, in 1782. Diego de Siloé took part in the plans. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The second tower was never built, and neither was the roof over the vaults. Water leaking in later damaged the interior, and urgent roof repairs began in 2009. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The north tower is 84 m high, or 92 m to the tip. Until 2012 only the Giralda in Andalusia was taller. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- Its vaults are 41.79 m high, the tallest of any cathedral in Andalusia. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The tower holds 14 bells. The 8 main ones were cast by Francisco Venero in 1784. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- *Note:* The inside needs a ticket; the question uses the outside façade only. The nickname 'La Manquita' (the one-armed lady) comes from the missing tower. A popular story says the tower money went to help American independence. That story is disputed and was not sourced, so it is left out of facts. Plaza del Obispo is a good place to view the façade. Very busy on cruise days.

**4. Plaza de la Merced (Picasso's square)** · Plaza de la Merced, 29012 Málaga (bench at the corner by No. 15, Casa Natal) · 36.72329722, -4.41750833

- Picasso was born in 1881 in the Casas de Campos (Nos. 15-20), now the Fundación Picasso Museo Casa Natal. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Merced_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- The seated bronze Picasso by sculptor Francisco López Hernández was placed in the square on 5 Dec 2008. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Merced_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- The obelisk (Rafael Mitjana, inaugurated 11 Dec 1842) has a crypt holding General Torrijos and his companions, shot on a Málaga beach in 1831 for trying to start a liberal uprising. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Merced_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- Isabella and Ferdinand's troops entered the city here on 19 Aug 1487, through the Puerta de Granada. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Merced_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- Excavations after the Victoria and Astoria cinemas were demolished (2019) found more than 300 skeletons from the conquest period, plus Roman burials. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Merced_(M%C3%A1laga)))
- *Note:* The Wikipedia article contradicts itself on the number of Torrijos's companions (48 or 47), so that number is left out. The Picasso bench is a photo queue magnet. The question is about his pose only; one source also calls it full-length, so check the exact pose on site. Leg to the Roman theatre: down Calle Alcazabilla, about 250 m.

**5. Teatro Romano (from Calle Alcazabilla)** · Calle Alcazabilla 8, 29015 Málaga · 36.72118868, -4.41687142

- The theatre dates from the early Empire. Its seats are cut into the Alcazaba hillside, Greek-style, and built up where there is no rock. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_romano_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- It was found by accident in 1951, during work on a garden. At first people thought it was a gate in the city walls. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_romano_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- For decades the Casa de la Cultura stood on top of it. It was demolished in the 1990s so the theatre could be dug out. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_romano_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The interpretation centre (architect Antonio Tejedor) opened in 2010 and shows fragments of the Lex Flavia Malacitana on its walls. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatro_romano_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- *Note:* You can see the theatre and the Alcazaba walls from the street at no cost. Entry to the site is run by the Junta de Andalucía; a search summary said it is currently free, but check. Check that the Lex Flavia text is still on the outer walls and readable from the street. Street musicians and crowds in the evening.

**6. Centre Pompidou Málaga (El Cubo)** · Pasaje Doctor Carrillo Casaux s/n, Muelle Uno, 29016 Málaga · 36.7189799, -4.4125449

- It was the first Centre Pompidou outside France. Spain's PM Mariano Rajoy and French culture minister Fleur Pellerin opened it on 28 Mar 2015. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centro_Pompidou_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The building, 'El Cubo', was designed by city architects Javier Pérez de la Fuente and Juan Antonio Marín Malavé. It covers about 7,600 m², of which about 6,300 m² is exhibition space. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centro_Pompidou_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- Muelle Uno opened as a shopping and leisure quay in Nov 2011, part of a plan to reconnect the port with the city. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muelle_Uno))
- The quay is old: its first stone was laid on 1 Jan 1588, under Philip II. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muelle_Uno))
- *Note:* Only the outside counts; the museum needs a ticket. The stop is reached through Paseo del Parque past the Aduana and the town hall. The leg from the Roman theatre is about 500-650 m. Many sources credit the coloured cube to Daniel Buren, but Wikipedia only says he had an exhibition there (2017-18), so he is not credited here.

**7. La Farola** · Paseo de la Farola, 29016 Málaga (inside the port, at the end of Muelle Uno) · 36.71414723, -4.41456823

- Engineer Joaquín María Pery y Guzmán built the lighthouse between 1813 and 1817. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Farola_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- For a century before that, a wooden lantern designed in 1717 by French engineer Bartolomé Thurus stood on the same spot. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Farola_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- On 28 Aug 1936 it was switched off because its silhouette helped enemy ships and planes find their targets. It was painted in earthy camouflage colours and rebuilt in 1939 after war damage. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Farola_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- The 1884 Andalusian earthquake put its light out of action, and it was repaired in 1885. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Farola_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- It was declared a Bien de Interés Cultural (monument) in 2023. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Farola_de_M%C3%A1laga))
- *Note:* The question as written can't be answered by looking. Replace it on site with a visible detail, for example a date on the plaque or the colour of the tower. The 'female name' line is a well-known tag, but no source was found, so check it. The lighthouse is in the port; you can walk all round its outside. La Malagueta beach is next door for a finish.

</details>

### Chiquito's Huelin Route · local

*Fistros, chimneys and sardines: the working-class seafront of west Málaga.*

Chiquito de la Calzada traffic light (Calle Tomás Echeverría x Calle Antonio Soler, Huelin) → Chimenea de los Guindos ('Torre Mónica'), Playa de la Misericordia · 6 stops · ≈ 3.0 km · 1.75 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** Huelin is Málaga's first purpose-built workers' quarter. Here tourists rarely go, and locals have an industrial past, a beloved comedian and an espeto (sardine skewer) culture, with every stop free and outdoors.

**Already offered:** Not found as a walking route. The city tourism site has generic coastal-path walks, and Chiquito's sites are mentioned piecemeal in press and travel blogs (e.g. https://www.escapadarural.com/blog/la-ruta-de-chiquito/).

**To fix:** 2 stop(s) without coordinates; a 1664 m leg; 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Chiquito de la Calzada's traffic light | Press the button on Chiquito's traffic light. When the light is red, which of his catchphrases do you hear? | ¡Quietor! | sourced |
| 2 | Parque de Huelin | This park is several streets from the sea, yet it has a structure you'd usually find on the coast. What is it? | A lighthouse | sourced |
| 3 | La Palera (Miquel Navarro sculpture) | This very tall seafront sculpture is called La Palera. In Andalusian Spanish a 'palera' is a type of plant. Which one? | Prickly pear cactus | check |
| 4 | Parque Gregorio Sánchez 'Chiquito de la Calzada' (bronze statue) | The park's name sign gives Chiquito's real name. What is his real first name? | Gregorio | sourced |
| 5 | Monumento al Espetero | The bronze espetero is doing Málaga's most famous beach cooking. What is on his skewer (espeto)? | Sardines | likely |
| 6 | Chimenea de los Guindos ('Torre Mónica') | This giant industrial chimney was built to resist acid fumes. What is it made of? | Brick | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Chiquito de la Calzada's traffic light** · Junction of Calle Tomás Echeverría and Calle Antonio Soler, Huelin, 29002 Málaga · None, None

- The ornamental traffic light was installed on 21 Jul 2022 at Tomás Echeverría x Antonio Soler. It does not control traffic. ([source](https://www.malaga.eu/visorcontenido/ANUDocumentDisplayer/166978/NOTAINFORMATIVA.pdf))
- A push button plays his phrases while the LED figure copies his gestures. On red it says '¡quietor!' and '¡pecador!'; on green, '¡al ataque!' and 'hasta luego, Lucas'. ([source](https://www.esdiario.com/andalucia/220722/98459/chiquito-vuelve-hacer-reir-malaguenos-cobra-vida-semaforo.html))
- A 2021 plan for a real traffic light saying 'jarl' was dropped. It could have been a risk for blind pedestrians, and it was expensive. ([source](https://www.eldiario.es/andalucia/malaga/malaga-queda-semaforo-diga-jarl_1_8444594.html))
- Chiquito, real name Gregorio Sánchez Fernández, was born on 28 May 1932 in Calzada de la Trinidad, the street behind his stage name, and died in Málaga on 11 Nov 2017. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiquito_de_la_Calzada))
- *Note:* The answer depends on sound. If the button is broken, the question fails, so check it on the walk and keep a backup visual question. Calle Tomás Echeverría is the barrio's main bar street. Nearest Metro: Princesa-Huelin. Check the exact distance from the station.

**2. Parque de Huelin** · Parque de Huelin, between Huelin and Jardín de la Abadía (Calle Héroe de Sostoa side), 29002 Málaga · 36.70602314, -4.43257463

- The park opened in 1992, built on land where 19th-century factories stood, including La Constancia, one of Spain's first blast furnaces. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huelin))
- It covers 32,000 m² and has more than 700 trees, lakes and an ornamental lighthouse. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parque_de_Huelin))
- Industrialist Eduardo Huelin Reissig proposed the barrio in 1868 as Málaga's first workers' quarter. Instead of crowded corralones, it was planned as small single-family houses, more than 1,000 of them. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huelin))
- Next to the park, the Iglesia de San Patricio parish was founded in 1891. The current building is from 1950, by architect Enrique Atencia, in a gothic-mudéjar revival style. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huelin))
- Each July the Virgen del Carmen leaves San Patricio, crosses the barrio, and is taken out to sea on a jábega rowing boat. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huelin))
- *Note:* The park has been fenced since 2014, so check opening hours if you go early or late. The lighthouse could also give a colour question once seen. San Patricio's exact address was not confirmed; one source places it near Calle Abogado Federico Orellana Toledano. Toilets and play areas are good for families.

**3. La Palera (Miquel Navarro sculpture)** · Paseo Marítimo Antonio Machado, Huelin seafront · None, None

- The tall sculpture on Paseo Marítimo Antonio Machado in Huelin is by Valencian sculptor Miquel Navarro, dated 2002. ([source](https://www.komoot.com/highlight/7274103))
- *Note:* The sources are thin: only Komoot. The meaning 'palera = chumbera/prickly pear' comes from general knowledge and needs checking. The sculpture's look and the exact position also need checking. The stop can be swapped or dropped if it doesn't work.

**4. Parque Gregorio Sánchez 'Chiquito de la Calzada' (bronze statue)** · Parque Gregorio Sánchez, next to Paseo Marítimo Antonio Banderas, Huelin · 36.693391, -4.442582

- The bronze statue by sculptor Ramón Chaparro is about 2 m tall, bigger than the real Chiquito (about 1.70 m), and weighs over 100 kg. It was unveiled in Dec 2021. ([source](https://elespanol.com/malaga/malaga-ciudad/20211211/nuevo-homenaje-chiquito-malaga-escultura-inaugurada-diciembre/633936799_0.html))
- He was 'Chiquito' because he started performing very young, and 'de la Calzada' after the street where he was born. He first sang flamenco as a child in a troupe called 'Capullitos Malagueños'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiquito_de_la_Calzada))
- He became a TV star at 62, after producer Tomás Summers signed him for Antena 3's 'Genio y figura'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiquito_de_la_Calzada))
- In 1985 he appeared briefly as a flamenco clapper (palmero) in an episode of the US series 'The Love Boat'. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiquito_de_la_Calzada))
- *Note:* Sources differ on the unveiling day: El Español gives 29 Dec as the planned date, and the council says 'December'. The day is left out. The park was remodelled in late 2023. A travel guide says the statue shows his signature pose, but no source describes it, so an alternative pose question could be added after the walk.

**5. Monumento al Espetero** · Paseo Marítimo Antonio Machado / Antonio Banderas, near Calle Pacífico (Playa de la Misericordia) · 36.695093, -4.439003

- The bronze monument to the espetero (the man who grills sardines on cane skewers over a beach fire) is about 2 m tall on a low stone base. It was inaugurated on 12 Jun 2006. ([source](https://intravel.net/malaga/attractions/estatua-del-espetero))
- *Note:* The sources are tourism sites, and they disagree on the promenade's name (Antonio Machado or Antonio Banderas). The sculptor was not found. Check the exact spot and what the figure holds. Many chiringuitos along this stretch sell espetos, a natural lunch stop.

**6. Chimenea de los Guindos ('Torre Mónica')** · Playa de la Misericordia, Paseo Marítimo Antonio Banderas (by the old Los Guindos lead foundry site) · 36.69253, -4.44026

- The chimney is 106 m tall and built of acid-resistant refractory brick, designed by German architect Félix von Schlippenbach for the Los Guindos lead foundry (built 1923). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chimenea_de_los_Guindos))
- Dictator Primo de Rivera inaugurated it in 1924. It carried toxic gases high into the air. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chimenea_de_los_Guindos))
- At its 1950s peak the foundry had 500 workers, mostly living nearby. It closed on 15 Nov 1979; the factory was demolished, but the chimney stayed. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chimenea_de_los_Guindos))
- Its nickname 'Torre Mónica' comes from a 1993 graffiti reading 'Mónica', removed in the 2007-08 restoration. A pendulum was then fitted at its base to detect earthquakes. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chimenea_de_los_Guindos))
- *Note:* Its foundations go 8 m deep because the site is so close to the beach. You can see it from a long way off, which helps the warm/cold mechanic on the last leg. The beach end is very exposed to sun.

</details>

## Palma de Mallorca

**Visitors:** No Palma-only count found. The Balearic Islands received 18,730,440 tourists in 2024 (overnight; day-trippers such as cruise passengers excluded – with them, visits would top 20 million). Palma airport handled a record 33,298,164 passengers in 2024.

**Official walking routes:** [Visit Palma – routes index (Palma Alta, Palma Baja/Modernisme, Jewish quarter, Gothic heritage, Palma for all, religious art, Cala Major)](https://www.visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes); [Visit Palma – A tour of Palma's old walls (Es Princep bastion to Sant Pere bastion/Es Baluard)](https://visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes/pln651/a-tour-of-palma-s-old-walls); [Visit Palma – Palma for all (accessible route)](https://visitpalma.com/en/dir/palma-for-all/)

**Notes:** Very strong summer seasonality (June–September peak, cruise days flood the old town around La Seu and the Born from mid-morning). July–August are hot; shade is scarce on Parc de la Mar and the seafront. Old town has steps (Costa de la Seu, steps down from the Almudaina to Parc de la Mar) – flag for buggies/wheelchairs. Santa Catalina/Es Jonquet sits on a low bluff with short climbs. Festivals: Sant Sebastià (city patron, around 20 January, concerts and bonfires in old-town squares), Festa de l'Estendard (31 December, Plaça de Cort), Sant Joan (23 June), Easter processions. Overtourism is a live political issue (2024 protests).

### The Olive, Turtles & Angels Route · classic

*From King Jaume's horse to the cathedral by the sea: turtles, a 600-year-old olive tree and angels on every roof.*

Plaça d'Espanya (Estació Intermodal: train, metro and bus hub) → Parc de la Mar (below the cathedral) · 8 stops · ≈ 3.5 km · 2.5 h · **ready to write**

**Why:** It walks a first-timer from the transport hub through the arcaded squares, Modernisme and the Born to the Gothic Llotja, La Seu and the sea, all downhill or flat and free outside.

**Already offered:** Yes. Visit Palma publishes old-town routes (Palma Alta / Palma Baja) at https://www.visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes; free and paid walking tours cover the same core (e.g. https://guruwalk.com/walks/17301-free-tour-palma-old-town); VoiceMap sells a cathedral/Jewish-quarter audio walk (https://voicemap.me/attraction/palma/placa-major-self-guided-audio-tours).

**To fix:** 1 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Monument to Jaume I | King Jaume I, who conquered Mallorca, is shown on what? | On horseback | sourced |
| 2 | Plaça Major | What surrounds this rectangular square on all sides? | Arcades (porticoes) | sourced |
| 3 | Plaça de Cort – the ancient olive tree | What kind of very old tree stands in front of Palma's City Hall? | An olive tree | sourced |
| 4 | Can Casasayas & Pensión Menorquina | These two near-identical Modernista buildings face each other. What separates them? | A narrow street | sourced |
| 5 | Font de les Tortugues (Turtle Fountain) | The obelisk of this fountain is held up by bronze animals. Which animals, and how many? | 4 turtles | sourced |
| 6 | Sa Llotja – the guardian angel | Above the main door of this Gothic exchange sits a statue protecting trade. What is it? | An angel | sourced |
| 7 | La Seu – Portal del Mirador | The sea-facing Portal del Mirador has a carved scene above the door. Which Bible scene is it? | The Last Supper | sourced |
| 8 | Parc de la Mar – ceramic mural | The huge ceramic mural at the end of the lake reproduces a work by which Mallorca-loving artist? | Joan Miró | check |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Monument to Jaume I** · Plaça d'Espanya, 07002 Palma · 39.576457, 2.654359

- The monument's central equestrian figure of King Jaume I is by Catalan sculptor Enric Clarasó, with an almogàver foot soldier holding a laurel branch. ([source](https://audiala.com/en/spain/palma/james-i-of-aragon))
- Clarasó did not win the original competition but still made the statue. ([source](https://www.loc.wiki/walking-trails/palma-de-mallorca-monuments-walk-01-99306506))
- The square was laid out from 1905, after Palma's city walls began to be demolished in 1902. ([source](https://www.mallorca.com/de/guide/sehenswuerdigkeiten/monument-a-jaume-i-el-conqueridor-monumento-a-jaume-i-el-conquistador))
- Enric Clarasó i Daudí (1857–1941) trained under Joan Roig in Barcelona. ([source](https://www.enciclopedia.cat/EC-GEC-0018067))
- *Note:* The monument's date is not confirmed by any source found (1927 unverified) – don't ask it. Busy traffic square; meet on the park side. Train/metro/bus station underneath.

**2. Plaça Major** · Plaça Major, 07001 Palma · 39.571378, 2.651725

- The site held the San Felipe Neri convent and later the seat of the Spanish Inquisition in Mallorca until 1823. ([source](https://www.mallorca.com/en/guide/sights/placa-major))
- The huge Inquisition building was demolished in 1823 to open the square. ([source](https://worldcitytrail.com/2025/01/28/placa-major-in-palma-de-mallorca/))
- The arcaded square was built from 1825 into the early 20th century. ([source](https://worldcitytrail.com/2025/01/28/placa-major-in-palma-de-mallorca/))
- Palma is planning a major redevelopment of the square after a design competition. ([source](https://amp.majorcadailybulletin.com/paperwork/living/2022/05/10/100967/mallorca-plaza-mayor-evolution.html))
- *Note:* Redevelopment works planned – check for hoardings before launch. Easy question; consider a harder one on site (e.g. count of entrances). Steps down from Plaça Major towards Carrer de Sant Miquel/Jaume II.

**3. Plaça de Cort – the ancient olive tree** · Plaça de Cort, in front of the Ajuntament (City Hall), 07001 Palma · 39.569615, 2.650064

- The olive tree came from the Pedruixella Petit estate near Pollença and was replanted here in 1989. ([source](https://travelfeed.io/@katrin-lux/a-600-year-old-olive-tree-as-a-symbol-of-peace))
- It is regarded as a symbol of peace and endurance. ([source](https://travelfeed.io/@katrin-lux/a-600-year-old-olive-tree-as-a-symbol-of-peace))
- The city council fenced it after delivery vans damaged branches; it is protected by the heritage catalogue commission. ([source](https://www.mallorcadiario.com/proteccion-olivera-de-cort))
- The City Hall has a baroque façade with carved wooden eaves and a clock nicknamed 'En Figuera'. ([source](https://ryo.co/en/destinations/spain/places/placa-de-cort/))
- *Note:* Age estimates vary (500–600 years per council official, 'over 600', even 'thousand-year-old') and size figures conflict – no age in facts or question. Plaça de Cort hosts the Festa de l'Estendard (31 Dec) and political demonstrations.

**4. Can Casasayas & Pensión Menorquina** · Plaça del Mercat 13–14, 07001 Palma · 39.571333, 2.648922

- Can Casasayas and the Pensión Menorquina are a pair of Art Nouveau buildings by Francesc Roca, completed by Guillem Reynés. ([source](https://www.spain.info/en/discover-spain/art-nouveau-palma-mallorca/))
- They were planned at the same time, and were meant to be joined by an upper walkway that was never built. ([source](https://visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/culture/pln515/can-casasayas-modernistische-symmetrie))
- They were commissioned by Josep Casasayas Casajuana, owner of the Can Frasquet cake shop. ([source](https://www.illesbalears.travel/en/mallorca/venue-can-casasayas-y-pension-menorquina))
- The two are separated by Carrer de Santacília. ([source](https://www.nomads-travel-guide.com/places/can-casasayas))
- *Note:* Dates conflict (1908–1909 vs 1908–1911) – omitted. The missing walkway is a great 'what's missing?' lesson hook. Gran Hotel (Domènech i Montaner, now CaixaForum) is a few metres away – bonus.

**5. Font de les Tortugues (Turtle Fountain)** · Plaça del Rei Joan Carles I, top of Passeig del Born, 07012 Palma · 39.568641, 2.652525

- A large circular basin supports an obelisk held up by four bronze turtles. ([source](https://www.waymarking.com/waymarks/wmF186_La_Font_de_les_Tortugues_Palma_Mallorca_Spain))
- An inscription dedicates it to Princess Isabel Luisa, dated 24 July 1833. ([source](https://www.waymarking.com/waymarks/wmF186_La_Font_de_les_Tortugues_Palma_Mallorca_Spain))
- It was moved in 1956, when Avinguda Jaume III was opened, to line up with the Born. ([source](https://casalsolleric.palma.es/en/-/history-buildings-general-direction-of-visual-arts-and-public-programs))
- The sphinxes along the Born were carved in 1833, at the same time as the turtle fountain. ([source](https://casalsolleric.palma.es/en/-/history-buildings-general-direction-of-visual-arts-and-public-programs))
- *Note:* Traffic roundabout – view from the Born pavement, don't cross into the roundabout. Sources disagree on the number of sphinxes on the Born (2 vs 4) – use only after counting on site. Casal Solleric (free art space) on the Born.

**6. Sa Llotja – the guardian angel** · Plaça de la Llotja, 07012 Palma · 39.568141, 2.644594

- Guillem Sagrera began the Llotja in 1426. ([source](https://www.enciclopedia.cat/gran-enciclopedia-catalana/llotja-de-mallorca))
- A guardian angel above the main door holds a banner naming it defender of trade. ([source](https://worldcitytrail.com/2025/01/29/llotja-de-palma/))
- Four octagonal towers stand at the corners, with smaller towers acting as buttresses. ([source](https://www.seemallorca.com/museums/la-llotja-palma-centre-and-marina-664083))
- Sagrera left before it was finished; other master builders completed it. ([source](https://worldcitytrail.com/2025/01/29/llotja-de-palma/))
- *Note:* Completion date varies (1447 vs 1452). Interior only open for exhibitions; question answered outside. Backup question: how many corner towers are octagonal (4).

**7. La Seu – Portal del Mirador** · Cathedral of Mallorca, south façade, Plaça de la Seu / Mirador terrace, 07001 Palma · 39.567473, 2.648168

- The Portal del Mirador is on the south façade, so called because it faces the sea. ([source](https://www.inspain.org/en/baleares/palma/la-seu-cathedral))
- Its carved decoration includes a bas-relief of the Last Supper, considered a Mallorcan Gothic masterpiece. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/majorca/things-to-do/palma-cathedral/))
- Guillem Sagrera's first documented job, in 1397, was cutting Santanyí stone for this portal. ([source](https://www.enciclopedia.cat/lart-gotic-a-catalunya/guillem-sagrera))
- Next door, the Almudaina palace's tallest tower has been called the Tower of the Angel since the 14th century, after the angel weathervane on top. ([source](https://enciclopedia.cat/gran-enciclopedia-catalana/castell-de-lalmudaina))
- *Note:* Cathedral interior is ticketed; the portal is viewed free from the terrace. Peak cruise crowds 10:00–14:00. Sources disagree on who made the Almudaina angel and when (Campredon 1310 vs Jaume II) – not used. Steps down to Parc de la Mar.

**8. Parc de la Mar – ceramic mural** · Parc de la Mar, Avinguda de Gabriel Roca / below the cathedral, 07001 Palma · 39.565852, 2.648489

- Parc de la Mar is a 9-hectare park on land reclaimed from the bay at the foot of the walls. ([source](https://www.nomadotravel.app/en/attractions/parc-de-la-mar))
- The park was inaugurated on 12 October 1984 after a long controversy about having more concrete than green. ([source](https://www.nomadotravel.app/es/attractions/parc-de-la-mar))
- Its lake is famous for the reflection of the cathedral. ([source](https://www.nomadotravel.app/es/attractions/parc-de-la-mar))
- *Note:* Mural authorship disputed: one guide says Miró donated a 1983 mural; another credits ceramist Lluís Castaldo with an 11 m, 200+ tile mural reproducing a late Miró work. Answer 'Joan Miró' fits both, but verify signage. Little shade.

</details>

### The Millers & Fishermen Route · local

*Windmills on a cliff, the oldest covered market and a neighbourhood theatre – Palma's old sailors' quarter.*

Es Baluard (Plaça de la Porta de Santa Catalina; several EMT bus lines on Passeig de Mallorca/Avinguda d'Argentina) → Els Rentadors, Es Jonquet (above Passeig Marítim) · 7 stops · ≈ 2.2 km · 2 h · **needs a site visit**

**Why:** Santa Catalina and Es Jonquet grew from fishermen's and millers' cottages outside the walls; their windmills, market and theatre tell a story most cruise visitors never hear.

**Already offered:** No dedicated official Santa Catalina/Es Jonquet route found; Visit Palma's routes index covers the old town and Cala Major (https://www.visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes), and its old-walls route ends at Es Baluard (https://visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes/pln651/a-tour-of-palma-s-old-walls). Private food tours visit the market.

**To fix:** short (~2.2 km); a 89 m leg (geofences may overlap); 4 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Es Baluard (Bastion of Sant Pere) | This museum sits inside a bastion of Palma's old city walls. Which saint is the bastion named after? | Sant Pere | check |
| 2 | Mercat de Santa Catalina | This is Palma's oldest covered market. In which decade did it open? | 1920s | check |
| 3 | Windmills of Carrer de la Indústria | How many old windmill towers line this street? | 4 | sourced |
| 4 | Església de Sant Magí (Immaculada Concepció) | Locals call this parish church 'Sant Magí'. What is its official dedication? | The Immaculate Conception | check |
| 5 | Teatre Mar i Terra | The neighbourhood theatre is called 'Mar i Terra'. What does that mean? | Sea and Land | sourced |
| 6 | Molí d'en Garleta, Es Jonquet | Es Jonquet's name comes from a plant that once grew here by the sea. Which one? | Reeds (rushes) | sourced |
| 7 | Els Rentadors (old washhouse), Es Jonquet | Els Rentadors was a shared neighbourhood building. What was it used for? | Washing clothes | likely |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Es Baluard (Bastion of Sant Pere)** · Plaça de la Porta de Santa Catalina 10, 07012 Palma · 39.570931, 2.642304

- Es Baluard occupies the Bastion of Sant Pere, part of the Renaissance fortifications that once enclosed Palma. ([source](https://www.nomadotravel.app/en/attractions/es-baluard-museum))
- The Santa Catalina gate in the walls was pulled down in 1914, opening the old city to the neighbourhood. ([source](https://edicioneslalibreria.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/02/Extracto-Santa-Catalina.pdf))
- Visit Palma's old-walls route ends at this bastion. ([source](https://visitpalma.com/en/discover/what-to-see-and-what-to-do/routes/pln651/a-tour-of-palma-s-old-walls))
- *Note:* Free access to the upper walkway/terraces is inconsistent across sources (museum ticket vs free 'Paseo de Ronda') – the question must be answerable from outside: check for a plaque naming the bastion. Sa Feixina park next door has the 1948 Crucero Baleares monument, under an open demolition dispute (Spain's government ordered removal in March 2026; courts had protected it) – avoid using it as a stop.

**2. Mercat de Santa Catalina** · Plaça de la Navegació s/n, 07013 Palma · 39.571231, 2.638265

- Opened in 1920 in the working-class, seafaring neighbourhood of Santa Catalina, it is Palma's oldest covered market. ([source](https://visitpalma.com/es/dir/mercat-de-santa-catalina/))
- Santa Catalina grew from the Es Jonquet settlement of fishermen, rope-makers and millers. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Catalina_(Palma_de_Mallorca)))
- The barri lies by the sea west of the old walled city and was settled early by fishermen and millers. ([source](https://www.enciclopedia.cat/EC-GEC-0058910))
- *Note:* Date is sourced, but needs a visible clue (plaque or date on the building) – otherwise reframe as a lesson fact. Mornings Mon–Sat; closed Sundays. Surrounding streets are now trendy restaurant strips.

**3. Windmills of Carrer de la Indústria** · Carrer de la Indústria (between Carrer de Sant Magí side and Son Espanyolet), 07013 Palma · 39.574755, 2.638454

- The four windmills on Carrer de la Indústria once ground grain; you can tell by their pointed round towers. ([source](https://www.mallorcamagazin.com/nachrichten/leben-und-stil/2025/01/12/131817/vom-winde-verweht-warum-auf-mallorca-viele-windmuhlen-gibt-und-kaum-noch-eine-betrieb-ist.html))
- A 2014 report headline: 'from seven mills to four'. ([source](https://www.mallorcamagazin.com/nachrichten/gesellschaft/2014/12/17/41129/aus-sieben-wurden-vier.html))
- Spanish Wikipedia cites the mills of Carrer Indústria and of Es Jonquet as evidence of the area's milling past. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Catalina_(Palma_de_Mallorca)))
- *Note:* Some mills are in private use/bars – count only towers. Busy street; walk the pavement. Leg from the market ~400–600 m (estimate).

**4. Església de Sant Magí (Immaculada Concepció)** · Carrer de Sant Magí, 07013 Palma · 39.570209, 2.63364

- Santa Catalina's 19th-century parish is the Church of the Immaculate Conception, also called Sant Magí. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Catalina_(Palma_de_Mallorca)))
- Traditional houses here are one or two storeys with terraces and Mallorcan shutters. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Catalina_(Palma_de_Mallorca)))
- *Note:* Thinly sourced stop: needs a visible inscription to work; otherwise replace with another Carrer de Sant Magí detail. Address number not found.

**5. Teatre Mar i Terra** · Carrer de Sant Magí 89, 07013 Palma · 39.570184, 2.63491

- The Societat Mar i Terra was founded in 1898; architect Josep Segura designed its building the same year. ([source](https://cultura.palma.es/en/qui-som-1))
- The building was the headquarters of the recreational 'Sea and Earth' Society. ([source](https://www.apropacultura.org/sites/default/files/teatre_municipal_mar_i_terra.pdf))
- Palma City Council has rehabilitated it as a municipal theatre. ([source](https://cultura.palma.es/en/qui-som-1))
- *Note:* Name sign assumed visible on the façade – confirm. Renovation works approved June 2026 (climate, lighting, ceilings) may mean scaffolding.

**6. Molí d'en Garleta, Es Jonquet** · Carrer del Molí d'en Garleta, Es Jonquet, 07013 Palma (street number varies by source) · 39.569937, 2.635895

- Es Jonquet is one of Palma's oldest quarters, small fishermen's houses protected as a Bien de Interés Cultural. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Jonquet))
- Its name refers to the rushes (jonc) that grew abundantly near the sea. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Catalina_(Palma_de_Mallorca)))
- Its flour mills are among the first things seen from ships entering the port. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Jonquet))
- The mills supplied bakeries in the neighbourhood and across Palma; Molí d'en Garleta was restored. ([source](https://www.palmaweekly.com/?p=3198))
- *Note:* Question is a lesson-style fact, not an on-site detail – replace with a visible one (e.g. count of sails/towers on the clifftop) after the walk. Sources disagree on mill ages (eldest 1406 vs 'since the 1400s') and on whether there were seven. Cobbled lanes and steps; not buggy-friendly.

**7. Els Rentadors (old washhouse), Es Jonquet** · Corner of Carrer de Cerdà and Carrer dels Gambins, Es Jonquet, 07013 Palma · 39.569855, 2.63749

- The Es Jonquet special protection plan covers the Rentadors on Carrer Cerdà and Carrer Gambins. ([source](https://intranet.caib.es/eboibfront/pdf/es/2023/153/1148850))
- After renovation the historic washhouse is planned as an exhibition space for artists living on Mallorca. ([source](https://www.mallorcamagazin.com/nachrichten/lokales/2025/02/21/133485/ausgehmeile-palma-mallorca-aus-historischem-waschhaus-wird-kunstzentrum.html))
- It once housed a tourist office. ([source](https://www.mallorcadiario.com/propuesta-pi-proposta-illes-apertura-oficina-turismo-palma-rentadors-jonquet))
- *Note:* Construction date unknown. Works likely – check. Viewpoints over the marina nearby; steps down to Passeig Marítim for buses.

</details>
