## Salamanca

**Visitors:** 735,546 hotel travellers and 1,173,734 overnight stays in 2025 (INE data published by the city tourism office), up 5.32% in travellers on 2024. No separate international figure found.

**Official walking routes:** [Ayuntamiento de Salamanca riverside and neighbourhood itineraries (recorridos 1-10: Tormes banks, Tejares, Huerta Otea, Chamberí, Arrabal, trades route)](https://www.aytosalamanca.es/documents/d/guest/b21_b_diptico_recorrido_1); [Paseo por la Galería Urbana (guided summer walk of Barrio del Oeste street art, from Plaza del Oeste)](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/calendar/visits/paseo-por-la-galeria-urbana); [City guided visits programme (Visitas guiadas a la ciudad)](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/uploads/documentosdescargables/109/Visitas_Guiadas_a_la_ciudad.pdf); [Ruta de los Miradores (13 viewpoints, 2026)](https://www.eldebate.com/espana/castilla-y-leon/20260728/salamanca-descubre-desde-arriba-nueva-ruta-miradores_444106.html); ['Alternative Salamanca' shopping route (city council)](https://saldecompras.aytosalamanca.es/en/publishes/trade-routes/alternative-salamanca-shopping-route); [Disfruta Salamanca app: Tormes banks and neighbourhoods](https://www.esmartcity.es/2024/03/15/app-disfrutasalamanca-permite-descubrir-riberas-rio-tormes-sus-barrios-forma-inmersiva)

**Notes:** Continental climate: hot dry summers (shade is scarce on the riverside), cold foggy winters. The old town sits on a hill above the Tormes: the classic route ends downhill at the Roman bridge, so the return is a climb. University term brings big student crowds at night (Plaza Mayor) and many school groups at the Patio de Escuelas frog. Holy Week processions (March/April) and the September fairs and fiestas (around 8 September) close streets around the cathedrals and Plaza Mayor; check dates. Train station is about 1.5 km north-east of the Plaza Mayor; bus station about 1 km north-west. salamanca-turismo pages were read via search summaries only. Some fact-to-URL attributions come from search summaries rather than opened pages; stop notes flag the doubtful ones.

### The Golden Stone Route · classic

*From a knight's tower to a Roman bridge, hunting a frog, an astronaut and a stone bull.*

Torre del Clavero, Calle Consuelo / Plaza de Colón → Puente Romano (city end, by the verraco) · 8 stops · ≈ 2.4 km · 2.5 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** Salamanca's best-known hidden details (the frog, the astronaut, the shells, the verraco) make it a natural treasure hunt, and they line up on a single downhill walk to the river.

**Already offered:** Yes, heavily. Free walking tours and the city's own guided visits cover Plaza Mayor, Casa de las Conchas, the University frog and the Cathedral astronaut; the frog/astronaut hunt is a standard tourist game. https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/uploads/documentosdescargables/109/Visitas_Guiadas_a_la_ciudad.pdf

**To fix:** short (~2.4 km)

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Torre del Clavero | The top of this tower is ringed by small round turrets. How many are there? | 8 | sourced |
| 2 | Plaza Mayor (Pabellón Real) | On the east side (the Pabellón Real), one king's figure takes the central place. Which king? | Fernando III 'the Saint' | sourced |
| 3 | Casa de las Conchas | Hundreds of stone scallop shells cover this house. Which other symbol, a flower, also appears on the façade? | The fleur-de-lis | sourced |
| 4 | Patio de Escuelas (University façade) | Search the University façade: a small animal sits on top of a skull. Which animal? | A frog | sourced |
| 5 | New Cathedral, Puerta de Ramos | Left of this cathedral door, a modern stonemason hid a figure among the old carvings. What is it? | An astronaut | sourced |
| 6 | Convento de San Esteban | In the big central relief of the church façade, what is happening to Saint Stephen? | He is being stoned | sourced |
| 7 | Huerto de Calisto y Melibea | This romantic garden is built on the slope of an old structure that once protected the city. What is it? | The medieval city wall | sourced |
| 8 | Puente Romano and the verraco | At the city end of the Roman bridge stands an ancient granite animal, older than the Romans. What animal is it? | A bull (verraco) | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Torre del Clavero** · Calle Consuelo / Plaza de Colón, Salamanca · 40.9628, -5.66329167

- It is one of the best-preserved Gothic towers in the Iberian Peninsula, though historians do not agree on who built it. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torre_del_Clavero))
- Its name comes from the post of 'clavero' (key-keeper) of the military Order of Alcántara held by its owner, traditionally Francisco de Sotomayor, around 1470. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torre_del_Clavero))
- The square base turns into an octagon as it rises, with little turrets at its eight corners linked by a gallery of small arches. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/torre-clavero))
- It was declared a national monument on 3 June 1931. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/torre-clavero))
- *Note:* Promoter disputed: Francisco de Sotomayor vs Diego de Anaya (who died by 1437), so no builder is stated as fact beyond the name's origin. Now offices of the Diputación; exterior only. Near the bus routes on Gran Vía / Paseo de Canalejas.

**2. Plaza Mayor (Pabellón Real)** · Plaza Mayor, Salamanca (east side, Pabellón Real) · 40.965, -5.66416667

- Works began in 1729 at the request of the corregidor Rodrigo Caballero Llanes, with a project drawn up by Alberto de Churriguera. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/plazas-mayores/plaza-mayor-salamanca))
- The Pabellón Real, the first side built, shows King Fernando III the Saint, and medallions in the spandrels of its arches show Spanish monarchs. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/plazas-mayores/plaza-mayor-salamanca))
- The medallion series has never been fully closed: in 2023 a new medallion honouring King Alfonso IX of León was added. ([source](https://www.eldiario.es/castilla-y-leon/provincias/salamanca/plaza-mayor-salamanca-luce-nuevo-medallon-homenaje-alfonso-ix-leon_1_10430690.html))
- It is considered the heart of Salamanca and one of the most beautiful squares in Spain. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_Mayor,_Salamanca))
- *Note:* Coordinates are for the square centre; the Pabellón Real is the east side (opposite the town hall is the north side). Sources disagree on the exact start day (10 March vs 10 May 1729), the finishing year (1753, 1755, 1756) and the number of medallions (88 is given once), so those are left out. Very crowded evenings; school groups. The year of the Alfonso IX medallion (2023) comes from the article's date and should be double-checked.

**3. Casa de las Conchas** · Calle Compañía 2 / Rúa Mayor, Salamanca · 40.962931, -5.665814

- Built between 1493 and 1517, it is a Gothic town palace with Plateresque touches. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_las_Conchas_(Salamanca)))
- It was built for Rodrigo Arias de Maldonado, a knight of the Order of Santiago and professor at the University; the façade has more than 300 shells, symbol of the order and of pilgrims to Santiago. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casa_de_las_Conchas))
- The fleurs-de-lis next to the shells belong to the Pimentel family of his wife. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/casa-de-las-conchas/))
- The top floor was rebuilt in the 18th century after cracks appeared, which is why it has no shells. ([source](https://www.spain.info/es/lugares-interes/casa-las-conchas/))
- *Note:* The fleur-de-lis / Pimentel detail comes from a search summary of a Salamanca guide (most likely lamaletainquieta.com); confirm the attribution. The façade also carries the Catholic Monarchs' coat of arms (English Wikipedia). Shell count is only ever approximate ('about 300', 'more than 300'), so it is not the question. The address 'Calle Compañía 2' is from general knowledge (the building is the public library); confirm. The courtyard is usually free to enter (library) but the question is on the outside.

**4. Patio de Escuelas (University façade)** · Patio de Escuelas, Calle Libreros, Salamanca · 40.96157222, -5.66751389

- The University of Salamanca is the oldest university still working in Spain and the Hispanic world, and the fourth oldest in Europe. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universidad_de_Salamanca))
- The main building, the Escuelas Mayores, was begun under the 1411 constitutions of Pope Benedict XIII (Papa Luna) and finished in 1533. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escuelas_Mayores))
- The frog sits on the left of three skulls on the big pilaster on the right as you face the façade. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/la-rana-de-salamanca/))
- The most common reading is a 'memento mori': the frog stands for lust and, sitting on death, reminds students that life is short. Tradition says spotting it brings good luck in exams. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/la-rana-de-salamanca/))
- The square is presided over by a statue of Fray Luis de León, who taught at this university. ([source](https://procomun.intef.es/en/media/96981))
- *Note:* Packed with groups; the frog is tiny, so the GPS radius should allow players to step back. The 'good luck in exams' line is widely repeated tradition; keep it framed as tradition. The date of the Fray Luis de León statue was not confirmed.

**5. New Cathedral, Puerta de Ramos** · Puerta de Ramos, Catedral Nueva, facing Plaza de Anaya / Calle Cardenal Pla y Deniel, Salamanca · 40.96069, -5.666

- The 'New' Cathedral stands alongside the Old one: Salamanca has two cathedrals side by side, seat of the diocese. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_Nueva_de_Salamanca))
- The astronaut was carved in 1992 when the Puerta de Ramos was restored for the 'Las Edades del Hombre' exhibition. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/astronauta-catedral-salamanca/))
- Next to it is a grinning dragon eating an ice-cream cone. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/astronauta-catedral-salamanca/))
- It is not an ancient mystery: restorers left a clearly modern symbol to mark which parts of the façade were new, following an old custom. ([source](https://leadstories.com/hoax-alert/2020/11/fact-check-the-astronaut-stone-carving-on-the-cathedral-of-salamanca-is-not-a-mystery.html))
- *Note:* Coordinates are Wikipedia's for the whole cathedral, not the door. Most sources credit the carving to stonemason Miguel Romero; one says Jerónimo García, so no name is given in facts. The figure has been damaged by bottles thrown at it and restored. The astronaut is on the left of the door as you face it; small, so a hint helps.

**6. Convento de San Esteban** · Plaza del Concilio de Trento, Salamanca · 40.960109, -5.662589

- The centre of the Plateresque façade shows the martyrdom (stoning) of Saint Stephen, with a Calvary above; both reliefs are by the Italian Juan Antonio Ceroni, early 17th century. ([source](https://www.urbipedia.org/hoja/Convento_de_San_Esteban_(Salamanca)))
- The façade was designed by Juan de Álava, Fray Martín de Santiago and Rodrigo Gil de Hontañón. ([source](https://homepages.bluffton.edu/~Sullivanm/spain/salamanca/esteban/esteban.html))
- Inside, the main altarpiece is by José Churriguera (late 17th century) and holds a painting of Saint Stephen's martyrdom by Claudio Coello. ([source](https://www.inspain.org/es/salamanca/salamanca/convento-de-san-esteban/))
- *Note:* Sources disagree on the start date (1524 vs 1525) and on where the Coello painting is (altarpiece vs façade), so these are kept out or worded carefully. Interior and cloister are ticketed; question uses the outside. Address from general knowledge; confirm. The stoning is easy to read; kids like spotting the stones.

**7. Huerto de Calisto y Melibea** · Calle Arcediano 20 (from the Patio Chico), Salamanca · 40.95936111, -5.66582222

- It is a 2,500 m² garden on the slope of the old city wall, above the river Tormes. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huerto_de_Calixto_y_Melibea))
- It is inspired by the garden in Fernando de Rojas's 'Tragicomedy of Calisto and Melibea' (La Celestina), the love story of Calisto and Melibea. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huerto_de_Calixto_y_Melibea))
- It opened on 12 June 1981. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huerto_de_Calixto_y_Melibea))
- There is no historical evidence that the story actually happened here. ([source](https://lamaletainquieta.com/huerto-calixto-melibea/))
- *Note:* Free entry but fenced: opens about 10:00 until sunset (one 2025 municipal PDF says 22:00), so it can be locked early in winter and at night. Sources give Calle Arcediano 20 (most) or 12. Step-free access. Lovers' padlocks on railings are temporary; do not use them for the question.

**8. Puente Romano and the verraco** · Puente Romano, city end at Plaza del Puente / Paseo del Rector Esperabé, Salamanca · 40.957783, -5.6702

- The bridge has 26 arches, of which only the first 15 date from Roman times. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_bridge_of_Salamanca))
- Floods in 1626 damaged two medieval arches while the Roman section survived; the bridge was closed to vehicles in 1973. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_bridge_of_Salamanca))
- The bridge and its stone bull are part of Salamanca's coat of arms; the bridge was declared an Artistic Historic Monument on 3 June 1931. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_bridge_of_Salamanca))
- The Verraco del Puente is a stone bull from the pre-Roman Vettones; its first documented placement at the bridge is from the 13th century, and by 1606 a stone bull featured in the city's coat of arms. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_bridge_of_Salamanca))
- In 2024 the city marked 50 years since Agustín Casillas's Lazarillo de Tormes sculpture was put in place. ([source](https://theobjective.com/cultura/2024-09-16/la-plaza-de-la-alamedilla-en-salamanca-llevara-el-nombre-de-agustin-casillas/))
- *Note:* Lesson idea (well known, not sourced here): in the novel 'Lazarillo de Tormes' the blind man knocks Lázaro's head against this stone bull; confirm before using. The Casillas sculpture is believed to stand by the bridge; confirm location. Length given as about 176 m by one source and about 350 m by the tourism office. The verraco is usually described as a bull, sometimes a boar; option wording 'bull (verraco)' covers both. End of route is at river level; return is uphill (about 10 min to the cathedrals).

</details>

### The Oeste Route · local

*From the hill where Salamanca was born to the garage-door gallery of the Barrio del Oeste and a house of skulls.*

Cerro de San Vicente (Corralón de San Vicente) → Palacio de Monterrey, Plaza de Monterrey · 6 stops · ≈ 2.0 km · 2.0 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It swaps cathedral crowds for the Iron Age hill where the city began, a neighbourhood that turned its garage doors into an open-air gallery, and quiet streets with Salamanca's spookiest façade.

**Already offered:** Partly. The tourism office runs a summer guided 'Paseo por la Galería Urbana' from Plaza del Oeste and the ZOES association runs SeptemberFest walks; Casa de las Muertes and Monterrey appear in general city tours. No combined route found. https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/calendar/visits/paseo-por-la-galeria-urbana

**To fix:** 1 stop(s) without coordinates; short (~2.0 km); a 90 m leg (geofences may overlap); 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cerro de San Vicente archaeological park | The first Salamancans lived here in the Iron Age. What shape were most of their houses? | Round | check |
| 2 | Plaza del Oeste | Look around the streets off this square: most of the neighbourhood's street paintings are done on what? | Garage doors | sourced |
| 3 | 'El juguetero' (Geppetto) wall mural | A giant painting covers the side wall of a building here. The old man in it is a famous character who made a wooden puppet. What is his trade? | Toymaker (woodcarver) | check |
| 4 | Campo de San Francisco | This is Salamanca's oldest garden. It is named after a convent of which religious order that once stood in front of it? | The Franciscans | sourced |
| 5 | Casa de las Muertes | This house is named after carved skulls ('muertes') on its upper windows. How many skulls are there? | 4 | sourced |
| 6 | Palacio de Monterrey | Big coats of arms sit on the corners of the towers. Which creatures hold them up? | Lions and griffins | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Cerro de San Vicente archaeological park** · Corralón de San Vicente s/n, Salamanca · 40.96407, -5.669073

- Excavations uncovered part of an early Iron Age village dating from the 7th to the 4th century BC, considered the origin of the city. ([source](https://www.jcyl.es/jcyl/patrimoniocultural/cerroSanVicente/web/assets/folleto-csv-web2.pdf))
- Around 400 BC its people moved to the neighbouring hill of the cathedrals (Teso de las Catedrales), where they built a walled hillfort. ([source](https://revistas.ucm.es/index.php/CMPL/article/download/95926/4564456569795/4564456716109))
- In 2021 the city council turned the hill's slopes into an archaeological park. ([source](https://www.elespanol.com/castilla-y-leon/region/salamanca/20210324/ayuntamiento-transforma-laderas-cerro-san-vicente-arqueologico/568445096_0.html))
- The museum on the hill offers free guided visits, and its story ends with the vanished Benedictine monastery of San Vicente. ([source](https://www.planomato.com/es/salamanca/v/museo-cerro-san-vicente))
- *Note:* 'Check': sources say most houses were circular (with some rectangular), built of mud and adobe, but it is not confirmed that round house remains or reconstructions are visible outdoors without the museum visit. If not, pick a visible detail on the walk (e.g. an outdoor panel). Museum hours vary. Exact attribution of the 'circular houses' line to a specific source still to confirm.

**2. Plaza del Oeste** · Plaza del Oeste, Barrio del Oeste (Carmelitas-Oeste), Salamanca · 40.97061, -5.667526

- The neighbourhood association ZOES started the street art in 2013 with a contest in which artists painted façades and doors with owners' and shops' permission. ([source](https://www.theculturemap.com/?p=9350))
- By 2022 the Galería Urbana festival was in its eleventh edition, with 18 artists from all over Spain painting mostly garage doors across eleven streets. ([source](https://sid-inico.usal.es/noticias/los-artistas-del-spray-compiten-en-el-barrio-del-oeste-de-salamanca/))
- The barrio is officially called Carmelitas-Oeste and is bounded by Avenida de Villamayor, Avenida de Portugal and Paseo de las Carmelitas; neighbours' initiatives here also created the Rastro de Salamanca flea market. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/monuments/barrio-del-oeste))
- Plaza del Oeste is the heart of the Galería Urbana, from which the artworks spread through nearby streets. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/monuments/barrio-del-oeste))
- *Note:* Individual murals change every year, so no question about a specific painting here. The often-quoted '504 garage doors, over 100 painted' figure could not be tied to a precise source, so it is left out. Lively bar terraces at night; quiet mornings.

**3. 'El juguetero' (Geppetto) wall mural** · Corner of Calle Papín and Calle Juan de Juni, Barrio del Oeste, Salamanca · None, None

- The tourism office highlights a large side-wall painting titled 'El juguetero o Geppetto' by the artist Mila Correch at Calle Papín with Juan de Juni. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/monuments/barrio-del-oeste))
- At the same corner is 'La Diosa Ciudadana' by Manuel Gutiérrez Calle. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/monuments/barrio-del-oeste))
- Many early murals were designed and painted by Fine Arts students. ([source](https://saladeprensa.usal.es/filessp/prensa/noticias/201307/CLIENT_111108772.pdf))
- *Note:* 'Check': murals can be repainted; confirm the Geppetto wall still exists and what it shows. Large side-wall (medianera) murals usually last much longer than garage-door works. If gone, swap to another current mural with a stable detail.

**4. Campo de San Francisco** · Campo de San Francisco, between Paseo de Carmelitas, Calle Ramón y Cajal and Calle Domínguez Berrueta, Salamanca · 40.966283, -5.668712

- The Campo de San Francisco is the oldest garden in Salamanca, created in the 18th century on a large plot in front of the convent of San Francisco el Real, which gave it its name. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/nature/campo-de-san-francisco))
- It has changed many times over the centuries; today it has a playground, an outdoor gym and a café terrace. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/nature/campo-de-san-francisco))
- In the 19th century the new Alamedilla park, opened in 1883, became more popular than the Campo de San Francisco. ([source](https://aytosalamanca.es/documents/d/guest/la-alamedilla-el-primer-parque-moderno-de-salamanca))
- *Note:* Answer is partly given away by the name; consider a visible-detail question chosen on the walk instead. One tourism page dates the Alamedilla to 1863 instead of 1883; the 1883 date comes from the tourism office's park page, and the attribution to the city history document is unconfirmed. Good toilet/coffee break.

**5. Casa de las Muertes** · Calle Bordadores s/n, Salamanca · 40.96587778, -5.66650278

- Four stone skulls stick out from the upper-floor windows, and locals call them 'muertes', which gave the house its name. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/casas-nobles/casa-muertes))
- It was built around 1500, most sources say by Juan de Álava for Archbishop Alonso de Fonseca. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/casas-nobles/casa-muertes))
- It was declared a Bien de Interés Cultural on 16 November 1983. ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Casa_de_las_Muertes))
- The next-door house of the Regidor Ovalle Prieto is where the writer Miguel de Unamuno lived. ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Casa_de_las_Muertes))
- *Note:* Legends of violent deaths in the house are generally treated as myth; one story ties the name to an 1835 killing. Architect attribution disputed (Juan de Álava vs Diego de Siloé vs Gil de Hontañón), so it is hedged. Narrow street; read the façade from across the road.

**6. Palacio de Monterrey** · Plaza de Monterrey / Calle Compañía, Salamanca · 40.965153, -5.666972

- It was commissioned in 1539 for the 3rd Count of Monterrey, Alonso de Acevedo y Zúñiga, to designs by Rodrigo Gil de Hontañón and Fray Martín de Santiago. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/palacios/palacio-monterrey))
- The pierced stone crest along the top is carved with nude men whose limbs turn into plants, and griffins; parts date from a 1950s restoration. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/palacios/palacio-monterrey))
- Only one of its four planned wings was ever built. ([source](https://www.turismocastillayleon.com/es/patrimonio-cultura/palacios/palacio-monterrey))
- It belongs to the House of Alba and is now also a museum. ([source](https://salamanca-turismo-prod.gvam.es/en/see/monuments/palacio-de-monterrey))
- *Note:* Leg from the Casa de las Muertes may be under 150 m on foot; measure, and if too short, merge or move this stop. The attribution of each palace fact to the Castilla y León page vs the city page is from search summaries; recheck. Route total may fall slightly under 2.5 km: measure on the ground, and if short, start from the Puente Romano river path (Paseo del Rector Esperabé) up to the Cerro.

</details>


---

## San Sebastián (Donostia)

**Visitors:** About 1,279,000 visits between September 2023 and August 2024 (Argia, citing City Council / Basque Government / Eustat / INE data). The city's 2025 tourism balance says arrivals and overnight stays were stable vs 2024 (slight drop under 1%), with 66.05% of overnight stays by international visitors. Eustat reports San Sebastián hotel entries +7.2% and overnight stays +9.5% in 2024. *No single official annual 'total tourists' figure for 2024 was found; figures above use different methodologies.*

**Official walking routes:** [Walking tours (official index)](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/plans-in-san-sebastian/walking-tours/); [Art & Nature route (about 10 km, 9 stops, 2h30)](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/plans-in-san-sebastian/walking-tours/art-nature/full-tour/); [Ruta Ciudad Marítima](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/ciudad-maritima/recorrido-completo/); [Parque Cristina Enea route](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/parque-cristina-enea/recorrido-completo/); [Accessible routes (incl. Woody Allen's San Sebastián, Cristina Enea)](https://accessibility.sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/routes/); [Open-air sculptures](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/open-air-sculptures/)

**Notes:** Atlantic climate: rain is the main issue, not heat. Big events: Tamborrada on 20 January (Plaza de la Constitución is its heart), Jazzaldia in July (concerts on Zurriola beach), Semana Grande/Aste Nagusia in mid-August (2024: 10-17 Aug), San Sebastián Film Festival in September (Kursaal). Old Town and La Concha get very crowded in July-August. Routes here are mostly flat; Monte Urgull/Igueldo are the hills (not used). Official tourist office: Ijentea 1. Note: research hit a web-search limit, so some quiz details are 'likely'/'check' and need the pre-launch walk.

### The Belle Époque Route · classic

*From the station bridge to the Queen's palace: casinos, numbered balconies and the most famous railing in Spain.*

Puente de María Cristina (next to Donostia/San Sebastián Renfe station, Estación del Norte) → Palacio de Miramar gardens (Miraconcha) · 7 stops · ≈ 3.9 km · 2.0 h · **ready to write**

**Why:** It strings the cathedral, Old Town, the casino-turned-city-hall and La Concha bay on one easy flat line from the main train station.

**Already offered:** Plenty of guided Old Town/La Concha walks exist (e.g. https://freewalkingtoursansebastian.com/ , Civitatis https://www.civitatis.com/en/san-sebastian/walking-tour/ ), the official site lists themed walking routes (https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/plans-in-san-sebastian/walking-tours/ ), and the treasure-hunt app My City Hunt has San Sebastián POIs such as the María Cristina bridge and Kursaal (https://mycityhunt.co.uk/cities/san-sebastian-es-10239/poi/maria-cristina-bridge-26954). No identical riddle route found.

**To fix:** 1 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Puente de María Cristina | Stand on the bridge and look at its corners. How many tall obelisks topped with sculptures guard it? | 4 | sourced |
| 2 | Catedral del Buen Pastor | Look up at the main façade. How many towers rise above the entrance porch? | 1 | sourced |
| 3 | Plaza de la Constitución | Look at the balconies around the square. What is painted on each one? | A number | sourced |
| 4 | Basílica de Santa María del Coro | The portal between the two towers is like a stone altarpiece. Which martyred saint stands at its centre? | San Sebastián | sourced |
| 5 | Ayuntamiento (former Gran Casino) | This city hall was built as a casino. How many towers stand on its main façade? | 2 | sourced |
| 6 | Barandilla de La Concha (La Concha railing) | Look closely at the white railing. Which decorative motif sits inside its circles? | A flower | sourced |
| 7 | Palacio de Miramar gardens | From the palace gardens, look out at the middle of the bay. What sits there? | An island with a lighthouse | likely |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Puente de María Cristina** · Puente de María Cristina over the Urumea, at Paseo de Francia by Estación del Norte (Renfe) · 43.3173, -1.9783

- Designed by engineer José Eugenio Ribera with architect Julio María Zapata; Turismo Euskadi compares it to the Pont Alexandre III in Paris. ([source](https://turismo.euskadi.eus/es/patrimonio-cultural/puente-de-maria-cristina/webtur00-content/es/))
- It has four obelisks at its ends, crowned by sculptural groups. ([source](https://worldcitytrail.com/2025/02/15/maria-cristina-zubia-san-sebastian/))
- Its three 24 m arches were an early use of reinforced concrete, with eclectic decoration influenced by the Vienna Secession (municipal heritage catalogue). ([source](https://www.donostia.eus/ide/PLANEAMENDUA_PLANEAMIENTO/shp/HOEBPB_2021_PEPPUC/pdf/ES/Grado%20F/GR%20F-9.pdf))
- In the 1980s the deteriorated bridge was rebuilt with a new cellular deck, but its decoration was kept so it looks the same. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puente_de_Mar%C3%ADa_Cristina))
- *Note:* Start at the Renfe station. Sources disagree on dates: Turismo Euskadi says built in 1904 in nine months; others give an inauguration on 20 January 1905 (San Sebastián day) - left out of facts. Sculptor attribution (Ángel García Díaz in one guide) not confirmed. Traffic on the bridge; cross at lights.

**2. Catedral del Buen Pastor** · Plaza del Buen Pastor, Centro (Calle Urdaneta / Calle Loiola) · 43.31669832, -1.98155766

- Its single tower above the entrance porch rises 75 metres as a needle spire, visible from almost everywhere in the city. ([source](https://turismo.euskadi.eus/es/patrimonio-cultural/catedral-del-buen-pastor/webtur00-content/es/))
- A neo-Gothic work by Gipuzkoan architect Manuel Echave, consecrated on 30 July 1897. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/san-sebastian/things-to-do/catedral-del-buen-pastor/))
- It is the largest religious building in Gipuzkoa and has been a cathedral only since 1953. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catedral_del_Buen_Pastor_(San_Sebasti%C3%A1n)))
- Its façade lines up with the Basílica de Santa María at the other end of Calle Mayor/Hernani/Loiola - you'll see the link at stop 4. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_del_Coro))
- *Note:* One source says works started in 1889. Spain.info credits the tower to Ramón Cortázar, others don't - left out. Leg to stop 3 runs straight down Calle Loiola/Hernani, crossing the Boulevard (Mercado de la Bretxa is on the way and could be a bonus stop).

**3. Plaza de la Constitución** · Plaza de la Constitución, Parte Vieja (Old Town) · 43.323613, -1.98489174

- The balconies are numbered because the square was used as a bullring; each balcony was rented as a box and its number identified it. ([source](https://descubredonostia.com/?p=6223))
- The neoclassical square was rebuilt after the great fire of 1813 and reopened in 1817. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/es/espana/san-sebastian/que-ver/plaza-de-la-constitucion-san-sebastian/))
- Its central building was the city hall until 1947 and then the municipal library (1951-2000). ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plaza_de_la_Constituci%C3%B3n_(San_Sebasti%C3%A1n)))
- It is the heart of the Tamborrada, the drum festival of 20 January, San Sebastián day. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/es/espana/san-sebastian/que-ver/plaza-de-la-constitucion-san-sebastian/))
- *Note:* Very busy with terraces and pintxos crowds in the evening and in summer. One source dates an earlier square by Hércules Torrelli to 1723 - single source, omitted. Arcades give rain shelter.

**4. Basílica de Santa María del Coro** · Calle 31 de Agosto 46, at the end of Calle Mayor, Parte Vieja · 43.32399104, -1.98669332

- A baroque basilica finished in 1774, with Gothic, Churrigueresque and neoclassical touches and a rococo-baroque portal. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_del_Coro))
- The portal, set between the two towers like an altarpiece, shows the martyred figure of San Sebastián, papal symbols and the city's coat of arms. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_del_Coro))
- Its main door is aligned with the Buen Pastor cathedral: turn around and look down Calle Mayor to see the spire. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_del_Coro))
- Works began in 1743, and it was given the rank of basilica in 1972. ([source](https://www.ecured.cu/Bas%C3%ADlica_de_Santa_Mar%C3%ADa_del_Coro))
- *Note:* Bonus question idea: 'Turn your back to the door: which spire do you see at the end of the street?' (Buen Pastor). Arrows on the saint's figure are not documented in sources - don't use them. Narrow street, crowded at peak times.

**5. Ayuntamiento (former Gran Casino)** · Alderdi Eder gardens, Ijentea kalea, facing La Concha · 43.32152744, -1.9857157

- It opened as the Gran Casino on 1 July 1887, with concerts and fireworks. ([source](https://www.hmdb.org/m.asp?m=234204))
- Designed by Adolfo Morales de los Ríos and Luis Aladrén; adapted as city hall in 1943 by Alday and Arizmendi, with the council moving in during 1947. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/san-sebastian/things-to-do/town-hall-donostia/))
- Its two church-like towers earned the gambling house the nickname 'Santa María de la Roulette'. ([source](https://www.guiarepsol.com/es/fichas/monumento/ayuntamiento-de-donostiasan-sebastian-19225/))
- It was the social centre of Belle Époque San Sebastián until gambling was banned in the 1920s. ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/en/to-do/culture-art-architecture/belle-epoque))
- *Note:* Sources disagree on the casino's closure (1923 vs 1924) and the move date (1945 vs 1947) - years omitted except 1947 from the stronger source. Alderdi Eder carousel is seasonal - not for questions.

**6. Barandilla de La Concha (La Concha railing)** · Paseo de La Concha, near Plaza Cervantes / La Perla · 43.317407, -1.98617

- Juan Rafael Alday designed the railing in 1910, replacing an 1895 railing by José de Goicoa; he also designed the 1911 lamp posts by the first ramp near Plaza Cervantes. ([source](https://donostia.eus/ide/PLANEAMENDUA_PLANEAMIENTO/shp/HOEBPB_2021_PEPPUC/pdf/ES/Grado%20F/GR%20F-4.pdf))
- King Alfonso XIII inaugurated it on 12 August 1916, unveiling a plaque and walking its 1,260 metres while the band played the 'Marcha de San Sebastián'. ([source](https://www.sansebastianturismoa.eus/es/blog/donosti-feeling/453-barandilla-de-la-concha))
- Anecdote: one stretch was fitted the wrong way round, so its flower faces the bay instead of the walkers. ([source](https://www.sansebastianturismoa.eus/es/blog/donosti-feeling/453-barandilla-de-la-concha))
- *Note:* Great mini-challenge: find the backwards section. Exact location of the inauguration plaque not confirmed - check on the walk (could be a better question). Sunny promenade, joggers and cyclists; families should keep to the pedestrian side.

**7. Palacio de Miramar gardens** · Paseo de Miraconcha, Miramar gardens (between La Concha and Ondarreta) · 43.31480822, -1.99852385

- The palace was built in 1893 at the request of Queen María Cristina. ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/establecimientos/palacio-de-miramar/))
- English architect Selden Wornum drew the project and master builder José Goikoa built it in English style with neo-Gothic touches. ([source](https://turismo.euskadi.eus/en/cultural-heritage/miramar-palace/webtur00-content/en/))
- The gardens were laid out by Pierre Ducasse, who also designed the Cristina Enea park. ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/parque-cristina-enea/))
- The island in the bay is Santa Clara; its old lighthouse has held Cristina Iglesias' sculpture 'Hondalea' since 2021. ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/isla-de-santa-clara/))
- *Note:* Gardens are public and free; palace interior is not a stop. Garden opening hours vary by season - check. Sources disagree on when Wornum got the commission (1888 vs 1889) - omitted. Bus back along the bay, or continue 1.5 km to Peine del Viento as an optional finale.

</details>

### The Tobacco to Surf Route · local

*From a cigar factory and a duke's peacock park to Moneo's glass rocks and the surfers' beach.*

Tabakalera (next to Donostia/San Sebastián Renfe station) → Paloma de la Paz, Paseo de Sagüés (Gros) · 6 stops · ≈ 3.2 km · 1.75 h · **needs a site visit**

**Why:** Egia and Gros are where locals live, surf and go to the Film Festival, yet most visitors never cross the Urumea.

**Already offered:** The tourism office publishes an Egia neighbourhood page and a Cristina Enea park route (https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/barrio-barrio/egia/ , https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/parque-cristina-enea/recorrido-completo/ ); the official Art & Nature route covers coastal sculptures like the Paloma de la Paz. My City Hunt lists the Kursaal. No combined Egia-Gros hunt found.

**To fix:** 4 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Tabakalera | This huge building's Basque name 'Tabakalera' tells you what was made here for 90 years. What was it? | Tobacco and cigars | likely |
| 2 | Parque de Cristina Enea | Which big, colourful bird roams freely around this park? | Peacock | likely |
| 3 | Iglesia de San Ignacio de Loyola | This is a neo-Gothic church. What shape are the tops of its doors and windows? | Pointed arches | likely |
| 4 | Kursaal and Zurriola bridge | How many giant translucent glass cubes make up Moneo's Kursaal? | 2 | sourced |
| 5 | Playa de la Zurriola | Look east along the beach. What rises at its far end? | Monte Ulía | likely |
| 6 | Paloma de la Paz (Dove of Peace) | What does this giant white sculpture facing the sea represent? | A dove | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Tabakalera** · Andre Zigarrogileak plaza 1, Egia (beside Estación del Norte) · 43.31658333, -1.97583333

- The new Tobacco Factory opened in Egia on 18 July 1913, after moving from a provisional site on Calle Garibay. ([source](https://revistaseug.ugr.es/index.php/erph/article/view/28431))
- It worked for 90 years, from 1913 to 2003, closing after the privatised tobacco company Altadis shut eight factories. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/es/espana/san-sebastian/que-ver/tabakalera/))
- It reopened in September 2015 as Tabakalera, the International Centre for Contemporary Culture. ([source](https://orain.eus/es/cultura/2015/09/11/tabakalera--entrevista-ane-rodriguez-directora-cultura/))
- Station, María Cristina bridge and Tabakalera form one heritage group on the Camino de Santiago. ([source](https://www.euskadi.eus/conjunto-estacion-del-norte-puente-de-mc-y-tabacalera/san-sebastian/camino-de-santiago/web01-a2donjak/es/))
- *Note:* Question is weak (name, not a visual detail). On the walk, look for a dated plaque or lettering on the façade and replace. Floor area conflicts (26,000 vs 37,000 m2) - omitted. Address 'Andre Zigarrogileak plaza 1' is from memory - check.

**2. Parque de Cristina Enea** · Cristina Enea park, Paseo Duque de Mandas, Egia (palace in the park) · 43.3141, -1.97591

- The park began as the private estate of the Dukes of Mandas, who started buying land in 1863; landscaping was by Pierre Ducasse. ([source](https://www.sansebastianturismoa.eus/es/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/parque-cristina-enea))
- Its name honours the duke's wife, Cristina Brunetti; Fermín de Lasala y Collado left the summer residence to the city (formalised in 1926). ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/parque-cristina-enea/recorrido-completo/))
- The palace's original layout dates from 1890 and is by José de Osinalde; it has housed the Cristina Enea Foundation since 2008. ([source](https://sansebastianturismoa.eus/hacer/planes-san-sebastian/rutas-a-pie/parque-cristina-enea/recorrido-completo/))
- *Note:* Peacocks are live animals, not a fixed detail - keep as a fun question but confirm they are still there; one source says they were introduced in the 1970s. Park has slopes and gates with opening hours - check. Leg 2-3 (about 1 km) crosses Egia toward Gros.

**3. Iglesia de San Ignacio de Loyola** · Plaza de Cataluña, Gros · 43.32333333, -1.975

- A neo-Gothic parish church in the Gros district. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iglesia_de_San_Ignacio_de_Loyola_(San_Sebasti%C3%A1n)))
- Gros was built on sandbanks east of the Urumea; it takes its name from architect Tomás Gros, whose ensanche dates from 1894. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gros_(San_Sebasti%C3%A1n)))
- Once largely industrial, Gros turned into a service district and is now one of the most valued parts of the city. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gros_(San_Sebasti%C3%A1n)))
- *Note:* Dates conflict (inaugurated 1897 vs completed 1898) - omitted. Architect not found. Question is generic; on the walk look for a dated stone or statue over the door to make it sharper.

**4. Kursaal and Zurriola bridge** · Avenida de Zurriola 1, Gros (at the Zurriola/Kursaal bridge) · 43.324444, -1.979082

- Rafael Moneo won the 1990 competition with the motto 'Dos Rocas Varadas' (Two Beached Rocks). ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Palacio_de_Congresos_y_Auditorio_Kursaal))
- Its walls are a double skin of translucent pressed glass on a complex metal frame. ([source](https://descubredonostia.com/en/the-kursaal/))
- Opened in 1999, it has been the main venue of the San Sebastián Film Festival ever since. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palacio_de_congresos_y_auditorio_Kursaal))
- The next-door Zurriola bridge, popularly 'Kursaal bridge', is famous for its conical lamp posts topped by huge lanterns. ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Palacio_de_Congresos_y_Auditorio_Kursaal))
- *Note:* Inauguration day conflicts (3 June vs 23 August 1999) - omitted. Bonus question: shape of the bridge lamps (conical with big lantern). Very crowded during the Film Festival (September).

**5. Playa de la Zurriola** · Paseo de Zurriola, Gros · 43.3264, -1.975

- The beach is about 800 m long, between the mouth of the Urumea and Monte Ulía. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playa_de_La_Zurriola))
- Before a 1994 reform with a new breakwater it was almost unusable because of the rough sea; afterwards it grew longer and became safe for bathing. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playa_de_La_Zurriola))
- It is the city's youngest-feeling beach, its main surf spot and a stage for Jazzaldia concerts. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playa_de_La_Zurriola))
- *Note:* Players can't read the mountain's name on site; consider adding a viewpoint sign check. Strong waves - keep kids off the water's edge in rough weather. Promenade is flat.

**6. Paloma de la Paz (Dove of Peace)** · Paseo de Sagüés, east end of Zurriola, Gros · 43.32812, -1.970732

- Néstor Basterretxea's Paloma de la Paz dates from 1988, commissioned by the City Council during years marked by terrorism. ([source](https://www.sansebastianturismoa.eus/images/documentos/recorrido-Costa-cas.pdf))
- It is made of white polyester to resist erosion and can withstand winds of up to 250 km/h. ([source](https://www.sansebastianturismoa.eus/images/documentos/recorrido-Costa-cas.pdf))
- It first stood by the Kursaal and in 1995 moved to Plaza Aita Donostia by Anoeta stadium. ([source](https://donostia.eus/ide/PLANEAMENDUA_PLANEAMIENTO/shp/HOEBPB_2021_PEPPUC/pdf/ES/Grado%20F/GR%20F-20.pdf))
- After a couple of moves it has faced the sea from the Sagüés promenade since 2015. ([source](https://www.deia.eus/bizkaia/2024/05/05/centenario-genio-inabarcable-8196004.amp.html))
- Basterretxea, born in Bermeo, died in 2014 aged 90. ([source](https://orain.eus/es/cultura/arte/2014/07/12/muere-basterretxea--muere-escultor-nestor-basterretxea-a-90-anos/))
- *Note:* Deia dates the work to 1998, conflicting with 1988 elsewhere; size (7 x 9 m) is single-source - both kept out except 1988 from the official brochure. Exposed, windy spot. Bus back to centre from Gros.

</details>

## Santiago de Compostela

**Visitors:** 942,928 tourists in 2024 by the conventional statistic (855,419 by an experimental method), per a USC/IDEGA report for the city's Sustainable Tourism Pact; the two methods differ a lot. Separately, the Pilgrim's Office issued almost half a million compostelas in 2024 (about 499,239), a record, and 58% of pilgrims were foreign.

**Official walking routes:** [Santiago Turismo route map: old town to the Bonaval and Belvís parks through San Pedro](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2016/07/roteirocdcmapaa3ingles.pdf); [Monumental Route of Santiago (Santiago Turismo)](https://issuu.com/santiagoturismo/docs/rutamonumentalen); [A Alameda guide (Santiago Turismo)](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/11/alamedainglesweb.pdf); [La ciudad escondida entre parques y jardines (Compostela Verde)](https://compostelaverde.santiagodecompostela.gal/wp-content/uploads/pdfs/info/arquivos/La ciudad escondida entre parques y jardines ESP.PDF); [Monuments leaflet (Santiago Turismo)](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/12/20221220154652_03FOLLmonuTURI3GAL.pdf)

**Notes:** Very rainy city: plan a rain version, and wet granite paving is slippery. 2027 is a Jacobean Holy Year (25 July falls on a Sunday), so expect record crowds and Holy Door queues in the Quintana all year; the Apostle's fiestas peak around 24-25 July. Obradoiro is packed around midday when pilgrims arrive. The old town is hilly but compact; the local route drops to the river Sar and back. santiagoturismo.com was only read through search summaries. Where a fact's URL is uncertain because it came from a search summary rather than an opened page, the stop's notes say so.

### The Pilgrim's Last Mile · classic

*Walk in through the pilgrims' gate, around the cathedral's four squares and out to the Alameda's view.*

Porta do Camiño (top of Rúa de San Pedro, where the Camino Francés enters) → Carballeira de Santa Susana, Parque da Alameda · 8 stops · ≈ 2.3 km · 2.5 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It retraces the final steps of the Camino Francés into the old town and circles the cathedral's squares before ending at the Alameda, the classic postcard view back over the city.

**Already offered:** Yes. Santiago Turismo's Monumental Route and countless free tours cover the cathedral squares; the Alameda is in the city's own guide. https://issuu.com/santiagoturismo/docs/rutamonumentalen

**To fix:** short (~2.3 km); 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Porta do Camiño | Pilgrims arriving on the Camino Francés enter the old town here. What stone monument stands at the entrance to Rúa de San Pedro? | A stone wayside cross (cruceiro) | likely |
| 2 | Praza de Cervantes | Whose bust crowns the fountain in the middle of this square? | Miguel de Cervantes | sourced |
| 3 | Praza da Inmaculada (San Martiño Pinario) | High on top of the monastery's main façade, a saint rides a horse and cuts his cloak to share it with a beggar. Who is he? | Saint Martin | sourced |
| 4 | Igrexa de San Francisco (Asorey monument) | In front of the church stands a big stone monument to Saint Francis by the sculptor Asorey. What shape is it? | A giant cross | check |
| 5 | Praza do Obradoiro (Pazo de Raxoi) | Read the inscription along the frieze of the Pazo de Raxoi (the long palace facing the cathedral). In which year did the works end? | 1772 | sourced |
| 6 | Praza da Quintana (via Praterías) | A wall plaque in this square honours a battalion of students who fought in the War of Independence (1808). What was the battalion called? | The Literary Battalion | sourced |
| 7 | As Dúas Marías (Parque da Alameda) | The colourful statue shows two sisters out on their daily walk. What is one of them holding? | An umbrella | sourced |
| 8 | Carballeira de Santa Susana | This oak grove surrounds a small Romanesque church on top of the hill. Which saint is it named after? | Saint Susana | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Porta do Camiño** · Praza da Porta do Camiño, at the entrance to Rúa de San Pedro, Santiago de Compostela · 42.882014, -8.540136

- The Camino Francés, before entering Santiago, runs along Rúa de San Pedro, where there is a church of the same name. ([source](https://www.pilgrim.es/santiago-de-compostela/san-pedro-de-fora/))
- The church of San Pedro is an 18th-century neoclassical building that keeps remains of the old monastery of San Pedro de Fora, outside the walled city at the entrance of the French Way. ([source](https://www.hmdb.org/m.asp?m=232706))
- The cruceiro at the entrance to Rúa San Pedro previously stood at Lavacolla. ([source](https://www.paxinasgalegas.es/cruceiro-porta-do-cami%C3%B1o-731676em.html))
- *Note:* Only a business-directory page places the cruceiro here, so 'likely'. Busy road junction; cross carefully. Leg to Praza de Cervantes is short (about 250 m) but over 150 m.

**2. Praza de Cervantes** · Praza de Cervantes, Santiago de Compostela · 42.881113, -8.542393

- The fountain crowned by a bust of Miguel de Cervantes is the square's best-known feature. ([source](https://explorial.com/es/?p=209241))
- The square was formerly called Plaza del Azougue or Plaza del Mercado, and was once the town's main market. ([source](https://explorial.com/es/?p=209241))
- The square's long history as a crossroads and market is the subject of a University of Santiago thesis, 'Del Campo a la ciudad: Historia urbana de Praza de Cervantes'. ([source](https://minerva.usc.gal/bitstreams/22f71058-a03f-41cf-aa61-c2f70a7fe830/download))
- *Note:* Answer is given away by the square's name: fine as an easy warm-up for kids, or swap. One regional listing says it was called 'El Foro' in the 12th century and that the town crier read ordinances here; not confirmed elsewhere, so left out. Date of the bust not found.

**3. Praza da Inmaculada (San Martiño Pinario)** · Praza da Inmaculada, between the cathedral's Azabachería façade and San Martiño Pinario, Santiago de Compostela · 42.88194444, -8.54444444

- San Martiño Pinario is a former Benedictine monastery founded in the 10th century; today it houses the seminary, a theology institute, the diocesan archive and a sacred art museum. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monasterio_de_San_Mart%C3%ADn_Pinario))
- It faces the cathedral's north (Azabachería) façade and covers 2.2 hectares. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monasterio_de_San_Mart%C3%ADn_Pinario))
- Its south façade, facing the Praza da Inmaculada, was designed by Fray Gabriel de Casas: four big Doric columns, Saint Benedict above the door, a large coat of arms of Spain and Saint Martin on horseback at the top. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monasterio_de_San_Mart%C3%ADn_Pinario))
- After Almanzor's raid on the city at the end of the 10th century, the monks founded a new monastery dedicated to Saint Martin of Tours. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monasterio_de_San_Mart%C3%ADn_Pinario))
- *Note:* Coordinates are Wikipedia's for the whole monastery. The church's separate west façade (on Praza de San Martiño) also shows Saint Martin on horseback; both work. Backup counting question: 4 Doric columns. The 'Azabachería' name comes from jet (azabache) workshops; not tied to a precise source here.

**4. Igrexa de San Francisco (Asorey monument)** · Praza de San Francisco / Rúa de San Francisco, Santiago de Compostela · 42.883526, -8.545076

- The Monument to Saint Francis of Assisi by Galician sculptor Francisco Asorey dates from 1926-1930. ([source](https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/monument-to-saint-francis-of-assisi-francisco-asorey/eAHkrz32rXFTJA))
- The monument appears on a teaching route through Asorey's work in Compostela. ([source](https://centros.edu.xunta.gal/cafi/aulavirtual/pluginfile.php/197211/mod_resource/content/1/RFACompostela.pdf))
- Tradition links the founding of the Franciscan convent to Saint Francis's own pilgrimage to Santiago, but this is legend, not documented fact. ([source](https://www.pilgrim.es/?p=42647))
- *Note:* 'Check': only one source calls it a gigantic cross and sources disagree whether it stands in the atrium or the porch. Confirm the shape and a clean visible detail on the walk. Church dating (mid-18th century) and the four-Doric-column façade come from one source only, so not used. Leg from Inmaculada about 200 m; then about 250 m to the Obradoiro.

**5. Praza do Obradoiro (Pazo de Raxoi)** · Praza do Obradoiro, west side (Pazo de Raxoi), Santiago de Compostela · 42.88055556, -8.54611111

- Archbishop Bartolomé Raxoi ordered the palace in 1766 as a seminary for confessors; the French engineer Carlos Lemaur designed it, and the frieze inscription dates the works 1766-1772. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazo_de_Raxoi))
- Today it houses the city hall and the presidency of the Galician government. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazo_de_Raxoi))
- Its façade shows the battle of Clavijo and, on top of the central pediment, Saint James on horseback (Santiago Matamoros) by José Ferreiro. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pazo_de_Raxoi))
- To its left is the Hospital Real of the Catholic Monarchs, built for pilgrims who needed care at the end of the Camino, designed by royal architect Enrique Egas and now a Parador hotel. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospital_de_los_Reyes_Cat%C3%B3licos))
- *Note:* Coordinates are Wikipedia's for the Pazo de Raxoi. Inscription is in Latin numerals/text: give a hint ('look for MDCCLXXII') and check legibility on the walk. One travel site gives 1776; English Wikipedia and the frieze give 1766-1772. Sources disagree whether the Clavijo scene is a relief in the pediment, so the facts word it loosely. Midday pilgrim crowds and frequent events.

**6. Praza da Quintana (via Praterías)** · Praza da Quintana, east side of the cathedral, Santiago de Compostela · 42.88055556, -8.54361111

- The square was built around 1611 on a medieval cemetery; the stairs divide it into the upper 'Quintana de Vivos' (of the living) and the lower 'Quintana de Mortos' (of the dead). ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quintana_Square))
- In Holy Years (when 25 July falls on a Sunday) pilgrims cross it to enter the cathedral through the Holy Door. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quintana_Square))
- A plaque commemorates the Literary Battalion, made up of students, during the Spanish War of Independence. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quintana_Square))
- On the way from the Obradoiro you pass the Praterías, named after silversmiths; since 1700 it has been split into the cathedral's south atrium and the square of the Fonte dos Cabalos (horse fountain). ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/12/03FOLLmonuTURI3PT.pdf))
- *Note:* Route from the Obradoiro goes down Rúa Fonseca and through the Praterías (horse fountain, 1825 per a travel guide; sources disagree on 2 vs 4 horse heads, so no count question there). Quintana is roughly 100 m from Praterías, too close for two stops. 2027 Holy Year: Holy Door queues fill the square. Night 'shadow pilgrim' illusion is lighting-dependent, do not use.

**7. As Dúas Marías (Parque da Alameda)** · Paseo da Ferradura / entrance of the Parque da Alameda, Santiago de Compostela · 42.877236, -8.546887

- Maruxa and Coralia Fandiño Ricart walked through the old town every day at two o'clock, when the cathedral's Berenguela bell struck, dressed in bright clothes and heavy make-up, from the 1950s to the late 1970s. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maruxa_and_Coralia_Fandi%C3%B1o_Ricart))
- Their family suffered persecution after the Civil War; their eccentric walks contrasted with the repression of the dictatorship and made them among the most famous women in Galicia. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maruxa_and_Coralia_Fandi%C3%B1o_Ricart))
- Sculptor César Lombera convinced mayor Xerardo Estévez in 1994, after nine years of proposals; the polychrome statue copies their best-known photo, with Maruxa's arm outstretched and Coralia holding an umbrella. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maruxa_and_Coralia_Fandi%C3%B1o_Ricart))
- César Lombera died in January 2025, aged 72. ([source](https://elespanol.com/treintayseis/cultura/20250127/galicia-dice-adios-cesar-lombera-autor-estatua-marias-alameda-santiago/919658385_0.html))
- *Note:* Sensitive backstory (repression, poverty, mental illness): keep the lesson respectful for families and schools. One source dates the statue to 1993; most say 1994 (unveiled on International Women's Day). Very popular photo spot. The two lions that open the Paseo dos Leóns are nearby (Santiago Turismo Alameda guide) and make a good bonus spot.

**8. Carballeira de Santa Susana** · Carballeira de Santa Susana, Parque da Alameda, Santiago de Compostela · 42.877871, -8.549475

- The park has three parts: the Paseo da Alameda, the Carballeira de Santa Susana and the Paseo da Ferradura, also called Paseo dos Leóns. ([source](https://www.gpsmycity.com/attractions/parque-la-alameda-(alameda-park)-61778.html))
- The Carballeira (oak grove) of Santa Susana is the oldest part, and the Alameda grew from it. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/11/alamedagalegoweb.pdf))
- There was once a monumental gate here guarded by two lions, the ones that now open the Paseo dos Leóns. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/11/alamedagalegoweb.pdf))
- The grove also holds a tribute to the Galician artist and politician Castelao next to the church of Santa Susana. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2022/11/alamedagalegoweb.pdf))
- *Note:* The three-parts fact is attributed to gpsmycity from a search summary; confirm. Easy answer (name of the grove), fine as a finale; swap if too easy. The Paseo da Ferradura gives the famous view of the cathedral, a good final photo. The 12th-century date of the church comes from an aggregator only, so left out. Unlit at night.

</details>

### The San Pedro to Sar Route · local

*Graves turned garden, a fake façade, the city's market and a church whose walls lean.*

Igrexa de San Pedro, Rúa de San Pedro → Colexiata de Santa María do Sar, Rúa do Sar · 6 stops · ≈ 2.6 km · 2.5 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It follows Compostelans rather than pilgrims: the bar street of San Pedro, Siza's park on an old cemetery, the convents and market where locals shop, and down to the quiet river Sar.

**Already offered:** Partly. Santiago Turismo publishes a route from the old town to the Bonaval and Belvís parks through San Pedro; Sar is not included. https://www.santiagoturismo.com/files/2016/07/roteirocdcmapaa3ingles.pdf

**To fix:** a 98 m leg (geofences may overlap); 3 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Igrexa de San Pedro (Rúa de San Pedro) | This church is outside the old walls on the pilgrims' street. Which saint is it named after, like the street itself? | Saint Peter | sourced |
| 2 | Parque de San Domingos de Bonaval | The top terrace of this park was once the convent cemetery. What is set among the trees and grass up there? | Old stone crosses and graves | sourced |
| 3 | Convento de Santa Clara (the 'fake façade') | This huge Baroque front is famous as a 'fake façade': its door does not open into the church. What is behind it? | A small garden courtyard | sourced |
| 4 | Mercado de Abastos | The market is built of long stone halls. How many halls (naves) are there? | 8 | check |
| 5 | Parque de Belvís | At the top of the hill above the park is an enclosed convent. Which nuns live there? | Dominican nuns | check |
| 6 | Colexiata de Santa María do Sar | Look at the outside of the north wall. Huge stone props were added centuries later to stop the walls from falling. What are they called? | Flying buttresses | likely |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Igrexa de San Pedro (Rúa de San Pedro)** · Rúa de San Pedro, small square by the church, Santiago de Compostela · 42.881631, -8.539095

- The neoclassical church of San Pedro, from the 18th century, keeps remains of the old monastery of San Pedro de Fora and stands outside the walled city at the entrance of the French Way. ([source](https://www.hmdb.org/m.asp?m=232706))
- It was built on the site of the 12th-century monastery of San Pedro de Fóra and is known for its sculptures by Ferreiro. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/monumentos/igrexa-de-san-pedro))
- Rúa de San Pedro is a cobbled street that today holds most of the city's bars and cultural life. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/monumentos/igrexa-de-san-pedro))
- *Note:* Weak, name-based question: replace on the walk with a visible detail (e.g. a dated stone or a Ferreiro statue). Lively street at night, quiet mornings. The second and third facts (Ferreiro sculptures, 12th-century monastery site, bar street) come from search summaries that did not name the exact page; they are attributed to the Santiago Turismo church page, but confirm.

**2. Parque de San Domingos de Bonaval** · Rúa da Ramírez / Campo de San Domingos de Bonaval, Santiago de Compostela · 42.8825, -8.53888889

- The park occupies the old orchards and cemetery of a Dominican convent founded traditionally by Saint Dominic during a supposed pilgrimage in 1219. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convento_de_Santo_Domingo_(Bonaval)))
- It was designed by Álvaro Siza with Isabel Aguirre and opened in 1994; it has three zones: orchard, cemetery and oak grove. ([source](https://www.paxinasgalegas.es/fiestas/parque-de-santo-domingo-de-bonaval-santiago-de-compostela-19809.html))
- In the lower orchard area stands Eduardo Chillida's sculpture known as the 'Porta da Música'. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/parques-e-xardins/parque-de-san-domingos-de-bonaval-7))
- The convent buildings now house the Museo do Pobo Galego, next to Siza's Galician Centre of Contemporary Art (CGAC). ([source](https://www.paxinasgalegas.es/fiestas/parque-de-santo-domingo-de-bonaval-santiago-de-compostela-19809.html))
- *Note:* Coordinates are Wikipedia's for the convent. Project dates disagree (1989-2000, 1990-?, 1987-1995 in the CCA archive), so only the 1994 opening is used. The Panteón de Galegos Ilustres (Rosalía de Castro's tomb) is inside the museum and ticketed. Park closes at night; stairs and slopes. The Siza/Aguirre, 1994 and three-zone facts and the Chillida location were read from search summaries; confirm the exact page for each.

**3. Convento de Santa Clara (the 'fake façade')** · Rúa de Santa Clara, Santiago de Compostela · 42.88527778, -8.54055556

- The convent of Poor Clares was founded in 1260, helped by a dowry from Violante of Aragon, wife of King Alfonso X the Wise. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convento_de_Santa_Clara_(Santiago_de_Compostela)))
- It was enlarged in the 16th century with gifts from Isabel de Granada, granddaughter of Boabdil, the last Moorish king, who was abbess here. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convento_de_Santa_Clara_(Santiago_de_Compostela)))
- The 'curtain' or fictitious façade by Simón Rodríguez, finished in 1719, leads only to the convent entrance and a small garden; the real, much plainer church front is at the end of the garden. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convento_de_Santa_Clara_(Santiago_de_Compostela)))
- Its style is Compostela 'plates' Baroque: geometric slabs and cylinders that seem layered on the wall, shapes suited to hard granite. ([source](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convento_de_Santa_Clara_(Santiago_de_Compostela)))
- *Note:* Enclosed convent; question is about what you see through the gate. Sources give 1717-1730 or 1719 for the façade; Wikipedia's 1719 completion is used. Uphill from Bonaval (about 350 m).

**4. Mercado de Abastos** · Rúa das Ameas, Santiago de Compostela · 42.879978, -8.541214

- The building was designed by architect Joaquín Vaquero Palacios in 1937 and completed in 1941, after the Civil War. ([source](https://www.mercasa.es/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/16_Mercados-de-Abastos_Santiago-de-Compostela.pdf))
- Its neo-Romanesque style matched the tastes of the new regime. ([source](https://www.mercasa.es/wp-content/uploads/2022/11/16_Mercados-de-Abastos_Santiago-de-Compostela.pdf))
- It is a granite complex inspired by Galician Romanesque buildings. ([source](https://www.paxinasgalegas.es/fiestas/plaza-de-abastos-de-santiago-de-compostela-santiago-de-compostela-19812.html))
- Researchers note that this market by the Asturian architect Vaquero Palacios (1900-1998) has still not been studied enough. ([source](https://revistaseug.ugr.es/index.php/sobre/article/view/31553))
- *Note:* 'Check': two tourist sources say eight naves (halls) but no technical source confirms; count on the walk. Construction dates differ (1937-1941 vs 1938-1942). Closed Sundays and busiest Saturday mornings; produce stalls change, so no stall-based questions. Address from general knowledge; confirm.

**5. Parque de Belvís** · Rúa das Trompas / Rúa de Belvís, Santiago de Compostela · 42.87791, -8.538488

- The park follows the small Belvís stream: a long space of meadows where old walls, terraces and paths such as the picturesque lane 'As Trompas' were kept. ([source](https://www.santiagoturismo.com/parques-e-xardins/parque-de-belvis-1))
- On the hilltop, the Convento de Belvís is home to a community of cloistered Dominican nuns known for baking sweets. ([source](https://www.trevorhuxham.com/2014/12/santiago-de-compostela-belvis-park.html))
- Beside it is the Seminario Menor, which also houses a pilgrim hostel. ([source](https://www.trevorhuxham.com/2014/12/santiago-de-compostela-belvis-park.html))
- *Note:* 'Check': not a visible detail; choose one on the walk (e.g. a dated stone on the convent or seminary). Sources are a blog and a tourism page via search summaries. Steep steps on Rúa das Trompas. Leg to Sar is about 900 m downhill; measure.

**6. Colexiata de Santa María do Sar** · Rúa do Sar s/n, Santiago de Compostela · 42.872123, -8.536968

- This 12th-century Romanesque church by the river Sar is famous for its leaning columns. ([source](https://www.spain.info/es/lugares-interes/colegiata-santa-maria-real-sar/))
- Sturdy flying buttresses were built in the 17th and 18th centuries to brace the north walls. ([source](https://flic.kr/photos/28944379@N02/53459840472))
- The cloister capitals are by the workshop of Master Mateo, the sculptor of the cathedral's Pórtico da Gloria. ([source](https://www.spain.info/es/lugares-interes/colegiata-santa-maria-real-sar/))
- Its cloister's north arcades date from the 13th century; the collegiate church has its own museum. ([source](https://museos.xunta.gal/en/museos/sar-collegiate-church-museum))
- *Note:* Leaning columns are inside (ticketed museum, listed as temporarily closed by the Xunta page), so the question uses the outside buttresses; confirm they are visible from the street. 'Likely' because the buttress fact comes from a photo caption. One source gives a founding date of 1136 by canon Munio Alfonso, unconfirmed, so left out. End point near the railway station area; bus back to centre.

</details>
