## Valencia

**Visitors:** No city-only 2024 figure found. The Valencian Community (region) recorded 11,938,668 international tourists in 2024, a record (up from ~10.5 million in 2023). *City-level numbers would come from INE / Visit València; not retrieved.*

**Official walking routes:** [Visit València – Walking tour through the historic centre (Serranos Towers to Quart Towers, ~2 h)](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/city-routes/historical-valencia); [Visit València – Getting around Valencia: the pleasure of walking (key walking routes)](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/city-routes/take-walk-valencia); [Ajuntament de València – Ruta guía de arquitectura: El Cabanyal (numbered building sheets)](https://www.valencia.es/cas/campa%C3%B1as-municipales/-/content/ruta-guia-arquitectura-el-cabanyal); [Visit València – The Cabanyal district](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-see-valencia/marine-villages-beaches-royal-navy/cabanal-neighborhood)

**Notes:** Completely flat city – ideal for families and school groups. July–August are hot and humid; plan early mornings/evenings and shade (Cabanyal has little shade near the beach). Las Fallas (15–19 March, build-up from 1 March) transforms the centre: Plaza del Ayuntamiento hosts the daily 2 pm mascletà, streets are closed and extremely crowded, and Plaza de la Virgen hosts the giant flower offering (17–18 March) – the classic route is near-unusable those days. Also: Corpus Christi procession, Feria de Julio, Semana Santa Marinera in Cabanyal (Easter). The Water Tribunal meets Thursdays at noon at the cathedral's Apostles' Door (great extra, crowds). The October 2024 DANA floods hit towns south of the city; check for any lingering works.

### The Parrot & Cannonball Route · classic

*Oranges, a talking parrot, cannonball scars and a river god – Valencia's old town in one flat stroll.*

Estació del Nord (Renfe; Metro L3/L5 Xàtiva next door) → Plaza Redonda (near Plaza de la Reina) · 8 stops · ≈ 3.8 km · 2.5 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It covers every icon a first-timer wants – the modernista station, City Hall square, Central Market, both medieval gates, the cathedral squares – with a playful, visible detail at each.

**Already offered:** Yes. Visit València publishes a self-guided historic-centre walk from Serranos to Quart Towers (https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/city-routes/historical-valencia); the regional tourism board lists paid 2-hour guided walks (https://www.comunitatvalenciana.com/en/valencia/valencia/experiences/regular-two-hours-walking-tours-through-the-historic-centre-of-valencia); GuruWalk free tours start at Plaza de la Virgen (https://www.guruwalk.com/walks/47936-essential-valencia-city-center-with-native-guide).

**To fix:** a 141 m leg (geofences may overlap); 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Estació del Nord | Which fruit decorates the façade of the North Station in garlands? | Oranges | sourced |
| 2 | Palacio de las Comunicaciones (old Post Office) – lion letterboxes | On a side façade of the old Post Office there are two historic letterboxes. What animal heads are they shaped as? | Lions | sourced |
| 3 | Mercado Central – the parrot weathervane | A famous weathervane on top of the market's dome is nicknamed 'la Cotorra del Mercat'. What bird is it? | A parrot | sourced |
| 4 | Torres de Quart | The stone of these towers is pockmarked with dozens of holes. What made them? | French cannonballs and gunfire in 1808 | sourced |
| 5 | Torres de Serranos | From the old riverbed side these towers look like a fortress. What do you see if you walk round to the city side? | The towers are open, like a stage with balconies | check |
| 6 | Plaza de la Virgen – Turia Fountain | Around the reclining river god Turia sit bronze women, one for each historic irrigation channel. How many women are there? | 8 | sourced |
| 7 | El Micalet (cathedral bell tower) | Look at the cathedral's bell tower, El Micalet. How many sides does it have? | 8 | sourced |
| 8 | Plaza Redonda | What stands right in the centre of the circular square? | A fountain | likely |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Estació del Nord** · Carrer de Xàtiva 24, 46007 València · 39.466133, -0.377772

- The station was designed by Demetrio Ribes (project 1906) and opened in 1917. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/ca/-/infociudad-estaci%C3%93-del-nord))
- Its creamy façade is decorated with bright ceramics, especially garlands of oranges. ([source](https://homepages.bluffton.edu/~sullivanm/spain/valencia/trainstation/ribes.html))
- The decoration celebrates Valencian agriculture: oranges, orange blossom, flowers and local tiles. ([source](https://www.nomadotravel.app/en/attractions/estacion-del-norte))
- It was declared a Monumento Histórico-Artístico in 1961. ([source](https://www.erih.net/i-want-to-go-there/site/northern-station))
- *Note:* The free-to-enter vestibule has famous 'bon viatge' mosaics – optional bonus. Busy taxi rank; gather the group on the Carrer de Xàtiva pavement. Plaza de Toros (bullring) is next door.

**2. Palacio de las Comunicaciones (old Post Office) – lion letterboxes** · Plaza del Ayuntamiento 24 (side façade), 46002 València · 39.469465, -0.375317

- The building was designed by Miguel Ángel Navarro Pérez of Zaragoza and built 1915–1922. ([source](https://www.visitvalencia.com/noticias/palacio-comunicaciones-edificio-correos))
- Its sculpture group with allegories of the five continents symbolises international communication. ([source](https://www.valenciabonita.es/2022/04/22/palacio-de-comunicaciones-valencia/))
- Two lion-head letterboxes on a side façade once took letters for the rest of Spain and abroad. ([source](https://www.visitvalencia.com/que-hacer-valencia/cultura-valenciana/monumentos-en-valencia/palacio-de-las-comunicaciones))
- The Generalitat bought the building, and since 2022 it has been visitable as the Palacio de las Comunicaciones. ([source](https://valenciaplaza.com/el-consell-firma-la-compra-del-edificio-de-correos-por-xx-millones))
- *Note:* Inauguration year varies (1922 vs 1923). During Fallas (1–19 March) the 2 pm mascletà fills the square – avoid. Inside visit is optional and free hours may change; the question is answered outside.

**3. Mercado Central – the parrot weathervane** · Plaça de la Ciutat de Bruges s/n, 46001 València · 39.473626, -0.379688

- The weathervane is called the Cotorra del Mercat (the market parrot); 'cotorra' also means chatterbox, for the bustle below. ([source](https://blog.visitvalencia.com/en/10-facts-of-the-mercado-central-you-did-not-know))
- Legend says the parrot gossips about the city with the sparrow weathervane (el Pardal) on the nearby Sants Joans church. ([source](https://www.valenciabonita.es/2015/09/23/las-3-veletas-del-mercado-central/))
- The main dome rises about 30 metres above the market floor. ([source](https://wdc2022.wdo.org/mercat-central-designed-for-eternity/))
- *Note:* Sources disagree on which dome holds the parrot and on the other weathervanes (swordfish vs dragonfly) – keep question to the parrot. Look up from Plaça de la Ciutat de Bruges; bring binoculars for kids. La Lonja de la Seda (UNESCO) is opposite – exterior free, interior ticketed. Market closed Sundays.

**4. Torres de Quart** · Carrer de Guillem de Castro 89 / Plaça de Santa Úrsula, 46001 València · 39.475899, -0.383927

- French artillery under Marshal Moncey bombarded the gate on 28 June 1808; the attackers were beaten back. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torres_de_Quart))
- The impact holes were deliberately kept during restorations as a memory of the siege. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torres_de_Quart))
- One count lists 132 cannonball marks and over 1,000 rifle-shot holes. ([source](https://en.northleg.com/valencia/sights/torres-de-quart/curiosities))
- Construction began in 1441. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torres_de_Quart))
- *Note:* Completion date disputed (1460 vs 1493) – only the 1441 start is used. Climbing inside is ticketed; the holes are visible on the outer (west) face from the street. Busy road crossing at Guillem de Castro.

**5. Torres de Serranos** · Plaça dels Furs s/n, 46003 València · 39.479281, -0.376174

- The gate was built between 1392 and 1398 under master builder Pere Balaguer. ([source](https://www.historyhit.com/locations/serranos-towers/))
- From 1586 it served as a prison for knights and noblemen, until 1887. ([source](https://www.historyhit.com/locations/serranos-towers/))
- Being in use as a prison saved it when most of the city walls were demolished in the 19th century. ([source](https://www.historyhit.com/locations/serranos-towers/))
- *Note:* The open-backed inner façade is well known but not confirmed in a source found here – mark 'check' and verify, or replace with a coat-of-arms question on site. Wall demolition year varies (1865 vs 'the 19th century'). Interior climb ticketed. Turia gardens directly in front for picnics.

**6. Plaza de la Virgen – Turia Fountain** · Plaça de la Mare de Déu, 46001 València · 39.476387, -0.375281

- The fountain is by Manuel Silvestre Montesinos ('Silvestre de Edeta') and opened in 1976. ([source](https://cultural.valencia.es/en/?p=143917))
- The central male figure represents the river Turia and the wealth its waters bring to the farmland (huerta). ([source](https://www.valencia.es/es/-/infociudad-monumentos-de-la-plaza-de-la-virgen))
- The eight women stand for the eight irrigation channels (acequias) fed by the river. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/valencia/things-to-do/plaza-de-la-virgen/))
- The Water Tribunal, which settles irrigation disputes, meets every Thursday at the cathedral's Apostles' Door on this square. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/valencia/things-to-do/water-tribunal/))
- *Note:* One source lists nine channel names (Benager and Faitanar are often counted as one) – don't ask for names. A council plaque on the square says 1977 but seems to relate to another dedication – 1976 used. Restoration announced in Feb 2025 – check fountain isn't fenced. Fallas flower offering (17–18 March) fills the square.

**7. El Micalet (cathedral bell tower)** · Plaça de la Reina, corner with Carrer de la Barcelonina, 46001 València · 39.474484, -0.375459

- El Micalet is an octagonal Gothic tower built 1381–1429, originally free-standing. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguelete_Tower))
- It is 51 m high to the terrace and 63 m including the bell gable; 207 steps lead to the top. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguelete_Tower))
- Its biggest bell, also called Micalet (after Saint Michael), weighs about 7.5 tonnes; the current one dates from 1532. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/valencia/things-to-do/micalet-valencia/))
- Anecdote: one guide notes that the tower's height and its perimeter are the same – 51 metres. ([source](https://www.barcelo.com/guia-turismo/en/spain/valencia/things-to-do/micalet-valencia/))
- *Note:* Bell counts differ (14 per Barceló vs 11 originals per Wikipedia) – not used. Climbing is ticketed; question answered from Plaza de la Reina. Plaza de la Reina was remodelled recently – check pedestrian layout.

**8. Plaza Redonda** · Plaça Redona, 46001 València (entrances from Carrer de la Pau side / Carrer de Sant Vicent Màrtir) · 39.473576, -0.3766

- Municipal architect Salvador Escrig built the square in 1840 on the site of the old main slaughterhouse. ([source](https://cultural.valencia.es/en/monument/ensemble-of-the-placa-redona/))
- Locals call it 'el Clot' – 'the hole'. ([source](https://www.spain.info/en/places-of-interest/plaza-redonda-valencia/))
- It was historically a market for fish and meat; small stalls and a Sunday market continue. ([source](https://www.spain.info/en/places-of-interest/plaza-redonda-valencia/))
- The square was rehabilitated in 2012. ([source](https://www.elespanol.com/valencia/20260304/plazas-bonitas-valencia-joya-siglo-xix-forma-circular-esnbspbien-relevancia-local/1003744152360_0.html))
- *Note:* The fountain's 1850 date appears only on tourism sites – not used. Fine end point: Plaza de la Reina and Mercado Central nearby. Narrow entrances; can be crowded on Sunday mornings.

</details>

### The Fishermen's Cabanyal Route · local

*Tiled houses, an oxen barn with two sundials, an ice factory and a fishermen's auction hall by the beach.*

Casa Montaña, Carrer de Josep Benlliure 69 (El Canyamelar; Metrovalencia/tram Marítim area – check nearest stop) → Lonja de Pescadores / Fábrica de Hielo, by Cabanyal beach · 6 stops · ≈ 2.8 km · 2 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** El Cabanyal is Valencia's old fishing village: its colourful tiled façades, fishermen's societies and beach heritage survived a decades-long demolition fight and are now protected, but most visitors only see the sand.

**Already offered:** Partly. The city council publishes a 'Ruta guía de arquitectura: El Cabanyal' with numbered building sheets (https://www.valencia.es/cas/campa%C3%B1as-municipales/-/content/ruta-guia-arquitectura-el-cabanyal); VoiceMap sells an audio walk (https://voicemap.me/tour/valencia/el-cabanyal-valencia-s-trendy-seaside-escape); the regional board describes 'Walking through Cabañal' (https://www.comunitatvalenciana.com/en/valencia/valencia/experiences/walking-through-cabanal).

**To fix:** 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Casa Montaña (bodega) | This tavern is one of the oldest in Valencia. In which year was it founded? | 1836 | check |
| 2 | Mercat del Cabanyal | Farmers still sell outside along one façade of this market at a spot with a special name. What is it called? | La Tira de Contar | check |
| 3 | El Casinet – Societat Musical Unió de Pescadors | The railings and part of the parapet of this 1909 fishermen's music society are made of curved bars of which material? | Cast iron | sourced |
| 4 | Casa dels Bous (Museu de la Mar) | The oxen house has a sundial on its south wall. What year is written on it? | 1895 | sourced |
| 5 | Lonja de Pescadores de la Marina Auxiliante | The fish auction hall's main façade faces a street named after whom? | The Men of the Sea (Hòmens de la Mar) | sourced |
| 6 | La Fábrica de Hielo | This 1925 building, now a cultural space, made something to keep the fish fresh. What was it? | Ice | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Casa Montaña (bodega)** · Carrer de Josep Benlliure 69, 46011 València (corner of three streets) · 39.471426, -0.329845

- The bodega was founded in 1836 and stands on a corner of three streets, main door on Josep Benlliure. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/8-casa-montana))
- The city heritage sheet says it is a typical neighbourhood building that stands out for its size and decoration. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/8-casa-montana))
- Inside, it keeps traditional ceramic tiles. ([source](https://valenciasecreta.com/en/casa-montana-2/))
- *Note:* Founding year is sourced, but whether it is written on the façade must be checked; if not, switch the question to a visible façade detail. It's a working bar (avoid group blocking the door; don't use as a 'shop name' question). Exact nearest metro/tram stop not verified.

**2. Mercat del Cabanyal** · Carrer de Martí Grajales s/n, 46011 València · 39.468428, -0.332436

- The first stone of the current market was laid in July 1958, after the great flood; the old market had stood on today's Plaza Doctor Lorenzo la Flor since 1869. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/20142/28910464/7.+Mercado+del+Cabanyal.pdf/9fb8987d-b63d-f79d-ce54-9cb19c4aac44?t=1721043402352))
- Farmers from the surrounding huerta still sell along the Martí Grajales façade, in the 'Tira de Contar'. ([source](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/gastronomy/markets/market-cabanyal))
- In 2022 the council awarded a €650,099.78 rehabilitation of façades and roofs, adding 48 LED spotlights. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/edusi3c/sites/default/files/docs/20220923_mercat-cabanyal-adjudicacion_val.pdf))
- *Note:* 'Tira de Contar' may not be signposted – answer depends on a sign or a lesson hint; mark 'check'. Alternative: count of doors on Martí Grajales (city report says triple entrance – verify). Morning is best; closed Sundays. Architect unknown.

**3. El Casinet – Societat Musical Unió de Pescadors** · Carrer de Josep Benlliure 272, 46011 València · 39.472165, -0.32956

- Built in 1909, 'El Casinet' is the popular name of the Societat Musical Unió de Pescadors building. ([source](https://cultural.valencia.es/en/?p=143882))
- It is considered one of the most emblematic Valencian modernista buildings in the neighbourhood. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/50-union-de-pescadores-building))
- Its railings and part of the parapet are curved cast-iron bars; the top has a central oculus with plant ornament. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/50-union-de-pescadores-building))
- Master builder Juan Bautista Gosálvez Navarro designed it; architect Víctor Gosálvez worked on it in 1919. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/50-union-de-pescadores-building))
- *Note:* Josep Benlliure street is lined with tiled façades – good for a 'spot the tiles' side task. Also on this street: water-themed 'Cares de l'aigua' details.

**4. Casa dels Bous (Museu de la Mar)** · Carrer dels Pescadors, 46011 València (street number varies by source: 7 or 42) · 39.470062, -0.331137

- The building housed the oxen that dragged fishing boats in 'bou' fishing (pair-trawling from the beach). ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/44-la-casa-dels-bous-and-les-tenyidores))
- After King Alfonso XII visited Valencia in 1877, he granted the fishermen permission to build a new Casa dels Bous. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/44-la-casa-dels-bous-and-les-tenyidores))
- It has two sundials: one on the south wall dated 1895, and a less visible one that never worked because of its orientation. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/44-la-casa-dels-bous-and-les-tenyidores))
- It is now the first site of the Museu de la Mar. ([source](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/valencian-culture/museum-in-valencia/museu-de-la-mar))
- *Note:* Build dates conflict (1877–1895 vs c.1880); one tourist source claims it held bulls for beach bullfights – contradicts the council, left out. Address number varies (7 vs 42 vs 'Eugènia Viñes 173'); geocode carefully. Museum entry not needed. Backup question: how many sundials (2).

**5. Lonja de Pescadores de la Marina Auxiliante** · Carrer d'Eugènia Viñes, corner Carrer dels Hòmens de la Mar, 46011 València · 39.466389, -0.325

- The Lonja is a large building on the beach whose long wings run parallel to Eugènia Viñes street. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/42-lonja-de-la-marina-auxiliante))
- Its main front faces Els Hòmens de la Mar street, with a frontispiece decorated with modernista motifs and a triglyph frieze. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/42-lonja-de-la-marina-auxiliante))
- The Marina Auxiliante society of boat-owners worked the southern part of the beach, while the Progreso Pescador society worked the north. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/45-la-fabrica-de-hielo))
- *Note:* Construction date (1904–1909) appears only in a secondary source – omitted. Not always open inside; view from outside. Plaza de los Hombres del Mar is a usual guided-tour start point.

**6. La Fábrica de Hielo** · Carrer de Pavía 37, 46011 València (council sheet places the complex on both sides of Eugènia Viñes) · 39.469509, -0.325072

- Built in 1925, the factory made ice to preserve fish both on boats and on land until sale. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/45-la-fabrica-de-hielo))
- It belonged to the heritage of the Sociedad Marina Auxiliante, the Cabanyal boat-owners' society. ([source](https://www.valencia.es/documents/d/guest/45-la-fabrica-de-hielo))
- Today it is a cultural venue with live music and events. ([source](https://www.visitvalencia.com/en/what-to-do-valencia/valencian-culture/cultural-places/la-fabrica-hielo))
- *Note:* Question is near-guessable from the name; make the lesson carry it, or swap for a visible façade detail found on the walk. Address inconsistency (Pavía 37 vs Eugènia Viñes) – geocode carefully. Beach is one block away: natural finish.

</details>


---

## Zaragoza

**Visitors:** Record 2024: 1,272,650 tourists (+8.9% vs 2023), including 392,953 international (+13.7%, about 30% of the total), and 2,133,762 overnight stays (+6%), per the City Council. A University of Zaragoza study using INE data gives 1,267,256 travellers and an economic impact of 682 million euros.

**Official walking routes:** [Old Town Route (Ruta del Casco)](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-y-hacer/rutacasco); [Mudéjar Route, World Heritage (about 2h30, from Plaza del Pilar office)](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-y-hacer/paseo-mudejar?locale=en); [Guided walks (incl. Los Sitios 1808-1809)](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/paseos-guiados?locale=en); [Public art itineraries (Casco Histórico, animals and plants, etc.)](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/casco/pilar); [15 neighbourhood routes on women's history](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/es-util/servicio/noticia/detail/302902)

**Notes:** Flat city. Summer heat is severe (July-August often very hot), and the cierzo wind is strong and cold in winter; plan shade and water, and avoid midday walks in summer. Biggest festival: Fiestas del Pilar around 12 October (Ofrenda de Flores fills Plaza del Pilar - expect closures and crowds). Tram line 1 runs along Avenida César Augusto. Note: research hit a web-search limit, so a few quiz details are 'likely'/'check'.

### The Pillar Route · classic

*From a Moorish palace to four bronze lions: siege scars, an emperor, a map-shaped fountain and the Pilar's four towers.*

Palacio de la Aljafería (bus/near Calle de los Diputados) → Puente de Piedra (lions at the Plaza del Pilar end) · 8 stops · ≈ 3.8 km · 2.25 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It runs the city's whole story, from Roman Caesaraugusta and the Taifa palace to the 1808 sieges and the Pilar, east in one line without doubling back.

**Already offered:** The tourist office runs an Old Town route and guided walks (https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-y-hacer/rutacasco), and My City Hunt lists Zaragoza hunt POIs such as the Puente de Piedra and the Pilar (https://mycityhunt.co.uk/cities/saragossa-es-10231/poi/puente-de-piedra-46358).

**To fix:** a 95 m leg (geofences may overlap)

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Palacio de la Aljafería (outside walls) | Walk along the moat and look at the outer towers. What shape are most of them? | Round | sourced |
| 2 | Puerta del Carmen | What can you still see pitting the stones of this gate? | Bullet and cannon-shot marks | sourced |
| 3 | Mercado Central | Look at the market's structure. Which two materials does this modernist building show off? | Cast iron and glass | sourced |
| 4 | Murallas romanas and Augustus statue | Which Roman emperor's bronze statue stands beside the wall? (Hint: he gave the city its name.) | Augustus | sourced |
| 5 | Fuente de la Hispanidad | Look at the shape of the pool and waterfall. Which part of the world is it a map of? | Latin America | sourced |
| 6 | Basílica del Pilar | Count the towers at the corners of the basilica. How many are there? | 4 | sourced |
| 7 | La Seo (Catedral del Salvador) - Parroquieta wall | Look at the Mudéjar outer wall of the Parroquieta chapel. What makes its geometric patterns? | Brick and glazed ceramic tiles | sourced |
| 8 | Puente de Piedra | What animals stand on tall pillars at the ends of this bridge, and how many are there? | 4 lions | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Palacio de la Aljafería (outside walls)** · Calle de los Diputados, Aljafería · 41.65649, -0.89708

- Today the palace is surrounded by a moat, thick walls and towers; the outer towers are rounded except the taller, rectangular Torre del Trovador. ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Palacio_de_la_Aljafer%C3%ADa))
- The palace was built in the 11th century for the Taifa king al-Muqtadir as a pleasure residence outside the city, among orchards and canals. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/palacio-de-aljaferia))
- The moat and bastions were added under Philip II by Tiburcio Spannocchi, in the late 16th century. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/pgou/edih/aljaferia.pdf))
- The Torre del Trovador is named after García Gutiérrez's 1836 romantic drama; the palace has housed the Aragonese parliament (Cortes de Aragón) since 1987. ([source](https://es.hispanopedia.com/wiki/Palacio_de_la_Aljafer%C3%ADa))
- *Note:* Interior visits need a ticket; the question uses the exterior only. Torre del Trovador dating varies (late 9th vs 10th century) - omitted. Leg 1-2 is about 1.1-1.2 km (longest leg) - verify the walking distance.

**2. Puerta del Carmen** · Plaza/Avenida César Augusto at Paseo de Pamplona · 41.649833, -0.887581

- Its walls still show impacts from the sieges: rifle holes and French cannon marks on the outer face, and only Aragonese rifle marks on the inner face. ([source](https://soydezaragoza.es/los-restos-los-sitios-zaragoza-quedan-la-ciudad/))
- The city says it was built in 1789 by architect Agustín Sanz and inaugurated in 1792. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/puerta-del-carmen))
- It is the only one of the city's twelve historic gates still standing, and was declared a protected monument in 1908. ([source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerta_del_Carmen))
- *Note:* Sources differ on construction dates (1789/1792 vs 1792-1795 in Artigrama) - the 1789/1792 claim is attributed to the city. Gate stands on a traffic island: cross at lights; good for families with care.

**3. Mercado Central** · Avenida César Augusto / Plaza Lanuza · 41.656065, -0.882892

- Félix Navarro designed it in 1895; works ran from January 1902 to June 1903 and it opened on 24 June 1903. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-hacer/servicio/monumento/187?locale=en))
- Navarro used the new materials of the day, especially cast iron and glass. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/casco/mercado))
- A restoration costing over 12 million euros and lasting just over two years brought back its modernist look; it reopened in February 2020. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/es-util/servicio/noticia/detail/229795))
- *Note:* In 2026 the city was still installing 126 windows in the enclosure - check for scaffolding. Market closed Sundays/evenings, but the question is about the exterior. Tram stop César Augusto in front.

**4. Murallas romanas and Augustus statue** · Avenida César Augusto, by the Torreón de la Zuda · 41.657181, -0.881841

- The statue is a bronze copy of the famous Augustus of Prima Porta. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/monumento/92))
- It was made in 1939-1940, promoted by Mussolini's Italian government, cast in Sestri Ponente and shipped from Genoa to Barcelona. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/191))
- The best-preserved stretch of wall, by the Torreón de la Zuda, is 80 m long with towers that are more than semicircular. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/murallas-romanas))
- The full circuit was about 3 km long and 10 m high, with around 120 towers and four gates. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/murallas-romanas))
- *Note:* When the walls were built is disputed (Augustan vs 2nd-3rd century AD) - left out of facts. Leg to the fountain is about 150 m (minimum length) - confirm.

**5. Fuente de la Hispanidad** · West end of Plaza del Pilar, next to San Juan de los Panetes · 41.657112, -0.880705

- The fountain was opened on 8 May 1991, ahead of the 500th anniversary of 1492. ([source](https://www.enjoyzaragoza.es/fuente-de-la-hispanidad/))
- It shows the outline of Central and South America, and three white-marble-clad concrete prisms stand for Columbus's caravels. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/noticia/328508))
- Water slides down a big slope into a sunken pool shaped like South America. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/66))
- The only place to see the whole map is from the Mirador de las 4 Culturas in the Torreón de la Zuda. ([source](https://www.enjoyzaragoza.es/fuente-de-la-hispanidad/))
- *Note:* Who designed it is disputed (Ricardo Usón per the council vs others) - omitted. Bonus: count the 'caravel' prisms (3). Water can be off for maintenance.

**6. Basílica del Pilar** · Plaza del Pilar · 41.656882, -0.878327

- The basilica has four corner towers and eleven domes on a 130 x 67 m footprint. ([source](https://fascinatingspain.com/art/monuments-of-spain/cathedrals/basilica-de-nuestra-senora-del-pilar/))
- Its domes are covered in green, yellow, blue and white glazed tiles. ([source](https://fascinatingspain.com/art/monuments-of-spain/cathedrals/basilica-de-nuestra-senora-del-pilar/))
- On 3 August 1936 two bombs were dropped on the basilica and did not explode. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/cont/paginas/turismo/pdf/29662%20%20Basilica%20Del%20Pilar%20ES.pdf))
- In 1772 a young Goya painted the dome above the small choir, opposite the Holy Chapel of the Virgin. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/ciudad/turismo/en/que-visitar/goya_elpilar.htm))
- *Note:* Free to enter, but the question is outside. Plaza very crowded at the Fiestas del Pilar (around 12 Oct). Architect attribution varies (Ventura Rodríguez vs others) - omitted. Bonus question: the colours of the dome tiles.

**7. La Seo (Catedral del Salvador) - Parroquieta wall** · Plaza de la Seo · 41.65456, -0.87585

- The San Miguel chapel ('Parroquieta') was built between 1374 and 1381; its brick and ceramic outer wall was basically finished by 1378. ([source](https://zaguan.unizar.es/record/153206/files/texto_completo.pdf))
- Archbishop Lope Fernández de Luna disliked the first ceramic work and, finding no Aragonese craftsmen to his taste, brought in an Andalusian (Sevillian) style. ([source](https://zaguan.unizar.es/record/153206/files/texto_completo.pdf))
- The apse, Parroquieta and lantern tower are part of the UNESCO World Heritage 'Mudéjar architecture of Aragon'. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-y-hacer/patrimonio-mundial))
- *Note:* Green-and-white tile colours are attributed in sources mostly to the lantern tower, not clearly to this wall - so not asked. UNESCO date confusion in sources (1986 Teruel listing vs 2001 extension) - omitted. Interior is ticketed; wall is on the outside.

**8. Puente de Piedra** · Puente de Piedra, at the Plaza del Pilar / Calle Echegaray end · 41.65722222, -0.87527778

- Four bronze lions by Francisco Rallo Lahoz were part of the bridge's reform, inaugurated on 8 May 1991. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/84))
- Each lion is about 250 cm tall on a pedestal of roughly 7 m. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/84))
- They are standing, not lying down, and are really one sculpture repeated four times. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/animales-plantas/puente-mercado))
- The bridge's old lions disappeared; one city itinerary says they were removed in the 1908 parapet works. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/sitios/piedra))
- *Note:* Windy over the Ebro (cierzo). Great sunset view back to the Pilar.

</details>

### The Lost Bell Route · local

*From El Gancho's octagonal tower to the bell that once guided lost travellers home, via a vanished leaning tower and a park that once kept bears.*

Iglesia de San Pablo, El Gancho (tram César Augusto) → Iglesia de San Miguel de los Navarros (near Plaza de España / Coso) · 7 stops · ≈ 3.0 km · 1.75 h · **needs a fix**

**Why:** It crosses El Gancho, the Magdalena and San Agustín, working-class quarters full of Mudéjar towers and local legends that most visitors skip after the Pilar.

**Already offered:** The tourist office's guided Mudéjar route covers La Seo, La Magdalena and San Gil (https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/ver-y-hacer/paseo-mudejar?locale=en), and the city's women's-history route takes in the Casco Histórico (https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/mujeres/casco); no hunt covering this exact line was found.

**To fix:** 2 question(s) to confirm on site

| # | Stop | On-site question | Answer | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Iglesia de San Pablo | Look at the slim brick bell tower. How many sides does it have? | 8 | sourced |
| 2 | Plaza de San Felipe (Torre Nueva) | A bronze boy sits in this square. What is he doing? | Looking up at where the leaning tower stood | sourced |
| 3 | Teatro romano de Caesaraugusta (street view) | Peer at the ruins from the street. What shape are the rows of seats arranged in? | A semicircle | likely |
| 4 | Iglesia de Santa María Magdalena | Look at the Mudéjar tower over the square. What shape is its base? | Square | likely |
| 5 | Centro de Historias (former convent of San Agustín) | Of the old convent church, what still faces the square? | Its façade and tower | sourced |
| 6 | Parque Bruil | Find the stone sculpture that imitates a wall. How many stone blocks make it up? | 13 | sourced |
| 7 | Iglesia de San Miguel de los Navarros | This tower's bell once guided lost travellers home at night. What shape is the tower's floor plan? | Square | sourced |

<details><summary>Stop details, facts and sources</summary>

**1. Iglesia de San Pablo** · Calle San Pablo, El Gancho (Barrio de San Pablo) · 41.65608333, -0.88611111

- The neighbourhood and parish are popularly called 'El Gancho', and the church is known as Zaragoza's 'third cathedral'. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/post/san-pablo))
- Begun in the late 13th century to replace the small Romanesque hermitage of San Blas, it was finished around 1343, when its octagonal Mudéjar tower was completed. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/monumento/8))
- Climbing the tower means 157 steps, no lift, to a viewpoint at 66 m; visits are guided only. ([source](https://zaragozala.com/turismo/torre-san-pablo-zaragoza-visitas-mirador/))
- Church and tower are part of the UNESCO-listed Mudéjar architecture of Aragon (2001 extension). ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/cont/paginas/turismo/pdf/PATRIMONIO%20MUNDIAL%20ESPANOL.pdf))
- *Note:* El Gancho is a working-class, multicultural area; fine by day. Street art from the Asalto festival exists in the area but was not verified, so no murals are used.

**2. Plaza de San Felipe (Torre Nueva)** · Plaza de San Felipe, Casco Histórico · 41.654652, -0.882013

- The Torre Nueva, a Mudéjar tower built in the early 16th century (1504), stood here until it was demolished in 1892. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/servicio/monumento/944))
- Its outline is traced in the paving of the square. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/casco/felipe))
- The bronze 'Boy sitting looking at the Torre Nueva' (1991) is by Santiago Gimeno Llop and gazes at the exact spot where the tower rose. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/153))
- *Note:* Bonus task: stand inside the tower's outline in the paving. Demolition year is 1892 per the city, 1893 per one local site. Quiet square; small and stroller-friendly.

**3. Teatro romano de Caesaraugusta (street view)** · Calle San Jorge 12 · 41.652172, -0.877658

- The theatre was discovered on 29 April 1972 during works for Ibercaja's headquarters, after an anonymous neighbour raised the alarm. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/museos/teatro/museo/))
- It was built in the first half of the 1st century AD, under Emperor Tiberius. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/cultura/patrimonio/servicio/edificio-historico/841))
- The museum opened on 9 May 2003; the site has been a protected monument (BIC) since 2001. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/cultura/patrimonio/servicio/edificio-historico/841))
- *Note:* Check how much of the cavea can be seen from the street without a ticket; the museum is paid. The 6,000-seat figure often quoted was not verified - omitted. Sources differ on which street the 1972 plot was on.

**4. Iglesia de Santa María Magdalena** · Plaza de la Magdalena · 41.6525, -0.87388889

- The city tourism office calls the church a real jewel of Zaragoza's Mudéjar art. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/monumento/12))
- Its square-based Mudéjar tower is its most striking feature. ([source](https://www.turismodearagon.com/ficha/iglesia-y-torre-de-santa-maria-magdalena-zaragoza/))
- Murals by Fabiola Gil and Vicente Gómez recreate lost city monuments, including the Puerta de Valencia on the Plaza de la Magdalena. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/arte-publico/itinerarios/mujeres/casco))
- The square was recently repaved to show off the setting of the Mudéjar church. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/noticia/318682))
- *Note:* Alternative question: 'Which lost city gate is painted on a wall here?' (Puerta de Valencia) - confirm the mural location. Lively bar square in the evenings.

**5. Centro de Historias (former convent of San Agustín)** · Plaza de San Agustín 2 · 41.650765, -0.870472

- The Augustinian convent was built here at the end of the 13th century and became a military supply barracks in the mid-19th century, after church property was seized. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/servicio/monumento/94?locale=en))
- Digs here found Iberian, Roman and Muslim remains from before the convent. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/servicio/monumento/94?locale=en))
- In the 2000-2003 works much of the church was demolished; only its 17th-18th century façade and tower were kept and are now the centre's main entrance. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/portal/exposiciones/centro-historias/edificio/antecedentes))
- The complex now holds the Centro de Historias and the María Moliner municipal library. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/es-util/servicio/noticia/detail/227662))
- *Note:* Address number '2' is from memory - check. Free exhibitions inside but question is outside.

**6. Parque Bruil** · Parque Bruil, San Agustín (by Calle Asalto) · 41.6499, -0.8685

- Juan Faustino Bruil bought the land in 1842, from the Augustinian convent's former grounds, and made it a garden with ponds, greenhouses and exotic animals. ([source](https://zaragozala.com/living-zaragoza/parque-bruil-zaragoza-historia/))
- The city took over part of the land in 1959 and opened the park on 18 June 1965; it includes the remains of an oil mill dated 1785. ([source](https://zaragozala.com/living-zaragoza/parque-bruil-zaragoza-historia/))
- The 1984 sculpture by Mariano Berges and Ángel Ibáñez is made of thirteen stone blocks. ([source](https://www.zaragoza.es/sede/servicio/arte-publico/100))
- A 2018 plaque against animal cruelty remembers the bears kept captive here from 1965. ([source](https://zaragoza.es/sede/portal/turismo/es-util/servicio/noticia/detail/227656))
- *Note:* IMPORTANT: sources say the park has been closed over health and decay problems, with a 20+ million euro plan and 100 new trees announced in Feb 2026 - check it is open; if not, skip this stop (the route still has 6). Street boundaries in the address are uncertain - geocode by park.

**7. Iglesia de San Miguel de los Navarros** · Plaza de San Miguel, by Calle Coso · 41.64956, -0.87646

- The tower is Mudéjar with a square plan; the oldest known document about the building, from 1396, mentions the tower being built. ([source](https://campaners.com/php/torrepdf.php?numer=2764))
- Its 'campana de los perdidos' (bell of the lost) rang to guide people who hadn't got back to the city before dark. ([source](https://zaragozala.com/living-zaragoza/campana-perdidos-zaragoza-san-miguel/))
- Today it rings 33 times at 22:07 in memory of the 'toque de perdido'. ([source](https://campaners.com/php/torrepdf.php?numer=2764))
- *Note:* Fun finale for evening groups: be here at 22:07 to hear the 33 rings (check the current schedule). Whether the bell guided the lost or announced the city gates closing is disputed - presented as tradition. Near Plaza de España tram/buses.

</details>
